**Lithuania**,[^cite_note-18] officially the **Republic of Lithuania**,[^cite_note-19] is a country in the [Baltic region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_region) of [Europe](9239.md).[^cite_note-location-28] It is one of three [Baltic states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_states) and lies on the eastern shore of the [Baltic Sea](3335.md), bordered by [Latvia](17514.md) to the north, [Belarus](3457.md) to the east and south, [Poland](22936.md) to the south, and the Russian [semi-exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclave) of [Kaliningrad Oblast](17311.md) to the southwest, with a [maritime border](29613061.md) with [Sweden](5058739.md) to the west. Lithuania covers an area of 65,300 km<sup>2</sup> (25,200 sq mi), and has a population of 2.9 million. Its capital and largest city is [Vilnius](32597.md); other major cities include [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas), [Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da), [Šiauliai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iauliai) and [Panevėžys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panev%C4%97%C5%BEys). [Lithuanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanians) are the [titular nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titular_nation), belong to the ethnolinguistic group of [Balts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balts), and speak [Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_language).&#x20;

For millennia, the southeastern shores of the Baltic Sea were inhabited by various [Baltic tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balts). In the 1230s, Lithuanian lands were united for the first time by [Mindaugas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindaugas), who formed the [Kingdom of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lithuania) on 6 July 1253. Subsequent expansion and consolidation resulted in the [Grand Duchy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Lithuania), which by the 14th century was the largest country in Europe. In 1386, the grand duchy entered into a *[de facto](43594.md)* [personal union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_union) with the [Crown of the Kingdom of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_of_the_Kingdom_of_Poland). The two realms [were united](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Lublin) into the [Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth) in 1569, forming one of the largest and most prosperous states in Europe. The commonwealth lasted more than two centuries, until neighbouring countries [gradually dismantled it](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partitions_of_Poland) between 1772 and 1795, with the [Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire) [annexing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Partition) most of Lithuania's territory.&#x20;

Towards the end of [World War I](4764461.md), Lithuania [declared independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_Independence_of_Lithuania) in 1918, founding the modern Republic of Lithuania. In [World War II](32927.md), Lithuania was occupied [by the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_occupation_of_the_Baltic_states_\(1940\)), then [by Nazi Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Lithuania_during_World_War_II), before being [reoccupied by the Soviets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_re-occupation_of_the_Baltic_states_\(1944\)) in 1944. [Lithuanian armed resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_partisans) to the Soviet occupation lasted until the early 1950s. On 11 March 1990, a year before the formal [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union), Lithuania became the first Soviet republic to break away when it [proclaimed the restoration of its independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_the_Re-Establishment_of_the_State_of_Lithuania).&#x20;

Lithuania is a [developed country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country) with a [high-income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy) and [an advanced economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Lithuania) ranking very high in [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index). Lithuania ranks highly in [digital infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_platform_\(infrastructure\)),[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30] [press freedom](8264708.md) and [happiness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Happiness_Report).[^cite_note-31] It is a member of the [United Nations](31769.md), the [European Union](9317.md), the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), the [Council of the Baltic Sea States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_the_Baltic_Sea_States), the [Eurozone](184391.md), the [Nordic Investment Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Investment_Bank), the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md), the [Schengen Agreement](179592.md), [NATO](21133.md), [OECD](33853117.md) and the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization). It also participates in the [Nordic-Baltic Eight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic-Baltic_Eight) (NB8) regional co-operation format.&#x20;

## Etymology

Main article: [Name of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Name_of_Lithuania)



Earliest mention of Lithuania (*Litua*, on line 7) in the entry for 1009 in the 11th-century *Annals of Quedlinburg*, Dresden, Sächsische Landesbibliothek, Q.113, fol 31r.

The spelling of *Lithuania* was a later addition to the original Latinate *Lituania* since 1800 as a form of [hyperforeignism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperforeignism) influenced by [Greek loanwords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_words_of_Greek_origin) with the [theta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theta); it is ultimately from [Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_language): *Lietuva*.[^cite_note-32] The first known record of *Lietuva* is in a 1009 story of [Saint Bruno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_of_Querfurt) in the *[Annals of Quedlinburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annals_of_Quedlinburg)*.[^cite_note-33] The chronicle records Latinized form of the name Lietuva: *Litua*.[^cite_note-34] The true meaning of the name is unknown, and scholars still debate it. There are a few plausible versions.[^cite_note-VardasVle-35]&#x20;

[Lietava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietava_\(Neris\)), a small stream near [Kernavė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kernav%C4%97)—the core area of the [early Lithuanian state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Lithuania) and a possible first capital of the eventual [Grand Duchy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Lithuania)—is usually credited as the source of the name.[^cite_note-Zigmas2-36] However, the stream is very small, and some find it improbable that such a small and localized body of water could have lent its name to an entire nation. On the other hand, such naming is not unprecedented in world history.[^cite_note-Zigmas-37]&#x20;

Artūras Dubonis proposed another hypothesis,[^cite_note-38] that Lietuva relates to the word *[leičiai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lei%C4%8Diai)* (plural of *leitis*). From the middle of the 13th century, *leičiai* were a distinct warrior social group of the [Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanians) society subordinate to the [Lithuanian ruler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Lithuania) or the state itself. The word *leičiai* is used in 14–16th century historical sources as an [ethnonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnonym) for Lithuanians (but not [Samogitians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samogitians)) and is still used, usually poetically or in historical contexts, in the [Latvian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_language), which is closely related to Lithuanian.[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40][^cite_note-41]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Lithuania)

### Early history and Baltic tribes

See also: [Lithuanian land confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_land_confederation)



Baltic amber was a valuable trade item, transported from the region of modern-day Lithuania to the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) through the [Amber Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amber_Road).

The history of Lithuania dates back to settlements founded about 10,000 years ago.[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-kudirka13-43] The first people settled in the territory of Lithuania after the [Last Glacial Period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_Glacial_Period) in the 10th millennium BC: [Kunda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunda_culture), [Neman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neman_culture) and [Narva cultures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narva_culture).[^cite_note-PoloméWinter2011-44] They were traveling hunters. In the 8th millennium BC the climate became warmer and forests developed. The inhabitants of what is now Lithuania travelled less and engaged in local [hunting, gathering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer) and fresh-water fishing. The [Indo-Europeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_migrations), who arrived in the 3rd – 2nd millennium BC, mixed with the local population and formed various Baltic tribes.[^cite_note-45] The [Balts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balts) did not maintain close cultural or political contacts with the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire),[^cite_note-MacDonald1996-46] while maintaining trade contacts via the [Amber Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amber_Road).&#x20;

From the 9th to the 11th centuries, coastal Balts were subjected to raids by the [Vikings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikings).[^cite_note-Kasekamp2017-47] Lithuania comprised mainly the culturally different regions of [Samogitia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samogitia) (known for its early medieval skeletal burials), and further east [Aukštaitija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auk%C5%A1taitija), or [Lithuania proper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_proper) (known for its early medieval cremation burials). The area was remote and unattractive to outsiders, including traders, which accounts for its separate linguistic, cultural and religious identity and delayed integration into general European patterns and trends.[^cite_note-Ochmański_37-48] Traditional [Lithuanian pagan customs and mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_mythology), with many archaic elements, were long preserved. Rulers' bodies were cremated up until the conversion to Christianity.[^cite_note-Bumblauskas_24-25-49]&#x20;

### Kingdom of Lithuania, Grand Duchy of Lithuania and Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth

Main articles: [Duchy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Lithuania), [Kingdom of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lithuania), and [Grand Duchy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Lithuania)

See also: [Lithuanian separatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_separatism)



Ancient [Kernavė hillforts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kernav%C4%97_Mounds)



[Trakai Island Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trakai_Island_Castle), the former residence of the [Grand Dukes of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Lithuanian_monarchs). Nearby [Senieji Trakai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senieji_Trakai) was the capital of the medieval state in 1316–1323, before the Lithuanian capital was transferred to [Vilnius](32597.md) by the Lithuanian monarch [Gediminas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas).



Changes in the territory of Lithuania from the 13th to 15th century. At its peak, Lithuania was the largest state in Europe.

[^cite_note-Bideleux-50]

[^cite_note-USDoS-51]

The first written record of the name for the country dates to 1009 AD.[^cite_note-52] Facing a [German threat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livonian_Crusade), [Mindaugas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindaugas) in the middle of the 13th century united a large part of the Baltic tribes and founded the State of Lithuania; in 1253, he was crowned the Catholic [King of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lithuania).[^cite_note-Mindaugas-53][^cite_note-LTHistoryBritannica-54] Lithuanian statehood was created on the basis of [Lithuania proper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_proper), where the [Lithuanians tribe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanians_\(tribe\)) lived and spoke the [Lithuanian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_language).[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56] Moreover, by taking advantage of the weakened territory of the former [Kievan Rus'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kievan_Rus%27) due to the [Mongol invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasion_of_Kievan_Rus%27), Mindaugas incorporated [Black Ruthenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Ruthenia) into Lithuania.[^cite_note-Mindaugas-53] After Mindaugas' assassination in 1263, pagan Lithuania was again a target of the [Christian crusades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Crusade) of the [Teutonic Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teutonic_Order) and [Livonian Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livonian_Order).[^cite_note-57] [Traidenis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traidenis) during his reign (1269–1282) reunified all Lithuanian lands and achieved military successes against the crusaders, fighting alongside other Baltic tribes, but was unable to militarily assist the [Old Prussians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Prussians) in their [Great Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prussian_uprisings#The_Great_Prussian_uprising_\(1260%E2%80%931274\)).[^cite_note-Traidenis-58] Traidenis' main residence was in [Kernavė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kernav%C4%97).[^cite_note-Traidenis-58]&#x20;

From the late 13th century, members of the [Gediminids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminids) dynasty began ruling Lithuania. Grand Duke [Gediminas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gediminas) consolidated a [hereditary monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hereditary_monarchy) and established [Vilnius](32597.md) as the [capital city](181337.md) in [his letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Letters_of_Gediminas). [Lithuania was Christianised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianization_of_Lithuania) and incorporated [East Slavs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Slavs)' territories (e.g. principalities of [Minsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Minsk), [Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Kiev), [Polotsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Polotsk), [Vitebsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Vitebsk), [Smolensk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Smolensk), etc.) significantly expanding the [Grand Duchy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Lithuania)'s territory to \~650,000 km2 in the first half of the 14th century.[^cite_note-VleLDK-59] At the end of the 14th century Lithuania was the largest country in [Europe](9239.md).[^cite_note-Bideleux-50][^cite_note-USDoS-51] In 1385, Lithuania formed a [dynastic union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynastic_union) with [Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Poland_during_the_Jagiellonian_dynasty) through the [Union of Krewo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Krewo).[^cite_note-VleLDK-59] By the 15th century [patrilineal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrilineality) [members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_dynasty) of the Lithuanian ruling Gediminids dynasty ruled Lithuania and Poland, as well as [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_\(1301%E2%80%931526\)), [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_in_personal_union_with_Hungary), [Bohemia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Bohemia), and [Moldavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moldavia).[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] Wars with the [Teutonic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_the_Teutonic_Order) in [1409–1411](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian%E2%80%93Teutonic_War) and [in 1422](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golub_War) concluded with the [Treaty of Melno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Melno).[^cite_note-VleLDK-59]&#x20;

In the 15th century the strengthened [Grand Duchy of Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Moscow) renewed the [Muscovite–Lithuanian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muscovite%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Wars) for Lithuanian-controlled [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md) territories.[^cite_note-VleLDK-59] Due to the unsuccessful beginning of the [Livonian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livonian_War), which resulted in loss of land to the [Tsardom of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsardom_of_Russia). Pressure by monarch [Sigismund II Augustus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigismund_II_Augustus) (a supporter of a close Polish–Lithuanian union), the [Lithuanian nobility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_nobility) agreed to conclude the [Union of Lublin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Lublin) in 1569 with the [Crown of the Kingdom of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_of_the_Kingdom_of_Poland), which created the [Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth) with a joint monarch, with Lithuania remaining a separate state.[^cite_note-LUnija-62][^cite_note-VleLDK-59] After concluding the [real union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_union), Lithuania and Poland jointly managed to reach [military successes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wars_involving_Lithuania) during the Livonian War, [occupation of Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_occupation_of_Moscow) (1610), [war with Sweden (1600–1611)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Swedish_War_\(1600%E2%80%931611\)), [Smolensk war with Russia (1632–1634)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smolensk_War), etc.[^cite_note-VleLDK-59][^cite_note-LUnija-62] In 1588, [Sigismund III Vasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigismund_III_Vasa) personally confirmed the [Third Statute of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutes_of_Lithuania#Third_Statute) where it stated that Lithuania and Poland have equal rights within the Commonwealth and ensured the [separation of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers).[^cite_note-63] The real union strongly intensified the [Polonisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonisation) of Lithuania and Lithuanian nobility.[^cite_note-64]&#x20;

The mid-17th century was marked with disastrous military losses for Lithuania as during the [Deluge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deluge_\(history\)) most of the territory of Lithuania was annexed by the Tsardom of Russia, and [Vilnius was captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Vilnius_\(1655\)) for the first time by a foreign army and ravaged.[^cite_note-Raila-65] In 1655, Lithuania unilaterally [seceded from Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_K%C4%97dainiai), declared Swedish King [Charles X Gustav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_X_Gustav) as the Grand Duke of Lithuania and fell under the [protection of the Swedish Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Lithuania).[^cite_note-66] However, by 1657 Lithuania was once again a part of the Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth following the Lithuanian revolt against the Swedes.[^cite_note-67] Vilnius was recaptured from the Russians in 1661.[^cite_note-68]&#x20;

In the second half of the 18th century, the Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth was [partitioned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partitions_of_Poland) three times by three neighbouring countries. This removed both independent Lithuania and Poland from the political map in 1795, following the failed [Kościuszko Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ko%C5%9Bciuszko_Uprising) and the short-lived [recapture of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_uprising_\(1794\)) in 1794.[^cite_note-VleLDK-59] Most of Lithuania's territory was [annexed by the Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Partition), while [Užnemunė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C5%BEnemun%C4%97) was annexed by [Prussia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Prussia).[^cite_note-VleLDK-59]&#x20;

### Efforts to restore statehood



[Emilia Plater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emilia_Plater) leading peasant [scythemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scythemen) during the 1831 Uprising against the Russian Empire; often nicknamed as a Lithuanian [Joan of Arc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joan_of_Arc)

[^cite_note-69]

Following the annexation the [Russian Tsarist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsarist_autocracy) authorities implemented [Russification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russification) policies in Lithuania, which then made a part of a new administrative region [Northwestern Krai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Krai).[^cite_note-Vle1795-1914-70] In 1812 [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon) during the [French invasion of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_invasion_of_Russia) established the puppet [Lithuanian Provisional Governing Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Provisional_Governing_Commission) to support his war efforts. After Napoleon's defeat the Russian rule was reinstated in Lithuania.[^cite_note-Vle1795-1914-70]&#x20;

During the [November Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_Uprising) (1830–1831) the Lithuanians and Poles jointly attempted to restore their statehoods, however the Russian victory resulted in stricter Russification measures: the [Russian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language) was introduced in all government institutions, [Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University) was closed in 1832, and theories that Lithuania had been a "Western Russian" state since its establishment were propagated.[^cite_note-Vle1795-1914-70] Subsequently, the Lithuanians once again tried to restore statehood by participating in the [January Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_Uprising) (1863–1864), but yet another Russian victory resulted in even stronger Russification policies with the introduction of the [Lithuanian press ban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_press_ban), pressure on the [Catholic Church in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Lithuania) and [Mikhail Muravyov-Vilensky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Muravyov-Vilensky)'s repressions.[^cite_note-Vle1795-1914-70][^cite_note-JIB-71] Lithuanians resisted Russification through an extensive network of [Lithuanian book smugglers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_book_smugglers), secret Lithuanian publishing and homeschooling.[^cite_note-72] Moreover, the [Lithuanian National Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_National_Revival), inspired by Lithuanian history, language and culture, laid the foundations for the reestablishment of an independent Lithuania.[^cite_note-73] The [Great Seimas of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Seimas_of_Vilnius) was held in 1905 and its participants adopted resolutions which demanded a wide [autonomy](191145.md#Politics) for Lithuania.[^cite_note-Vle1795-1914-70]&#x20;

### Restored statehood and occupations

See also: [Occupation of the Baltic states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_the_Baltic_states) and [Guerrilla war in the Baltic states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guerrilla_war_in_the_Baltic_states)



Members of the Council of Lithuania after signing the Act of Independence of Lithuania in the [House of the Signatories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_the_Signatories) in 1918

During [World War I](4764461.md) the [German Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Empire) annexed Lithuanian territories from the Russian Empire and they became a part of *[Ober Ost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ober_Ost)*.[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74] In 1917, the Lithuanians organised the [Vilnius Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Conference) which adopted a resolution, featuring the aspiration for the restoration of Lithuania's sovereignty and military alliance with Germany and elected the [Council of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Lithuania).[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74] In 1918, the short-lived [Kingdom of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Lithuania_\(1918\)) was proclaimed; however on 16 February 1918 the Council of Lithuania adopted the [Act of Independence of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_Independence_of_Lithuania) which restored Lithuania as a democratic [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic) with its capital in Vilnius and without any political ties that existed with other nations in the past.[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74]&#x20;

In 1918–1920 Lithuanians defended the statehood of Lithuania against [Bolsheviks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian%E2%80%93Soviet_War), [Bermontians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian%E2%80%93Bermontian_War) and [Poles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_War) during the [Lithuanian Wars of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Wars_of_Independence).[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74] The Bolsheviks supported their [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state) [Socialist Soviet Republic of Lithuania and Belorussia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Soviet_Republic_of_Lithuania_and_Belorussia).[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74] The Lithuanian authorities prevented the [1919 Polish coup attempt in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1919_Polish_coup_attempt_in_Lithuania).[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74] During the [Spa Conference of 1920](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa_Conference_of_1920) the Polish prime minister [Władysław Grabski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_Grabski), seeking for the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_I) help against the Bolsheviks before the decisive [Battle of Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Warsaw_\(1920\)), signed an agreement by which Vilnius was given up to the Lithuanians and the Polish Army was to be retreated to the [Curzon Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curzon_Line) of 8 December 1919.[^cite_note-75] However, the aims of the newly restored [independent](62450.md) Lithuania clashed with the Polish chief of state [Józef Piłsudski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B3zef_Pi%C5%82sudski)'s plans to create a federation ([Intermarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermarium)) in territories previously ruled by the [Jagiellonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_dynasty).[^cite_note-76] Later in 1920 the [Suwałki Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suwa%C5%82ki_Agreement) was concluded between Lithuania and Poland, however the next day the [Żeligowski's Mutiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BBeligowski%27s_Mutiny) began with Piłsudski's backing and soon the Polish forces captured [Vilnius Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Region), including capital city Vilnius, which was unsuccessfully defended by the heavily outnumbered Lithuanian Army soldiers, and established a puppet state of the [Republic of Central Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Central_Lithuania), which in 1922 was incorporated into Poland.[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74][^cite_note-77] Consequently, [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas) became the [temporary capital of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temporary_capital_of_Lithuania) where the [Constituent Assembly of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constituent_Assembly_of_Lithuania) was held and other primary Lithuanian institutions operated until 1940.[^cite_note-78] Nevertheless, Lithuania's independence and [sovereignty](28791.md) were preserved in the Wars of Independence.[^cite_note-Vle1918-1920-74]&#x20;

In 1923, the [Klaipėda Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da_Revolt) was organised which unified the [Klaipėda Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da_Region) with Lithuania.[^cite_note-Vle1920-1940-79] The [1926 Lithuanian coup d'état](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1926_Lithuanian_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat) replaced the democratically elected government and [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazys_Grinius#President_\(1926\)) with an authoritarian regime led by [Antanas Smetona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antanas_Smetona).[^cite_note-Vle1920-1940-79]&#x20;



[Lithuanian Armed Forces](17827.md) returning to Vilnius in 1939

In the late 1930s Lithuania accepted the [1938 Polish ultimatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1938_Polish_ultimatum_to_Lithuania), [1939 German ultimatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1939_German_ultimatum_to_Lithuania) and transferred the Klaipėda Region to [Nazi Germany](21212.md). The 1939 [Soviet–Lithuanian Mutual Assistance Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Mutual_Assistance_Treaty) allowed the presence of Soviet troops in Lithuania.[^cite_note-VleListorija-80] In 1940 Lithuania accepted the [Soviet ultimatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_ultimatum_to_Lithuania) and recovered the control of its historical capital Vilnius, however, the acceptance resulted in the [Soviet occupation of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_occupation_of_the_Baltic_states_\(1940\)) and its transformation into the [Lithuanian Soviet Socialist Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic).[^cite_note-VleListorija-80] In 1941 during the [June Uprising in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_Uprising_in_Lithuania) it was attempted to restore independent Lithuania and the [Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Army) was expelled from its territory, however in a few days Lithuania was [occupied by Nazi Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_occupation_of_Lithuania_during_World_War_II).[^cite_note-VleListorija-80] In 1944 Lithuania was [re-occupied by the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_re-occupation_of_the_Baltic_states_\(1944\)), and [Soviet political repressions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_repression_in_the_Soviet_Union) along with [Soviet deportations from Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_deportations_from_Lithuania) resumed.[^cite_note-VleListorija-80] Thousands of [Lithuanian partisans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_partisans) and their supporters attempted to militarily restore independent Lithuania, but their resistance was eventually suppressed in 1953 by the [Soviet authorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chronology_of_Soviet_secret_police_agencies) and their [collaborators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extermination_battalion).[^cite_note-VleListorija-80] [Jonas Žemaitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_%C5%BDemaitis), the chairman of the [Union of Lithuanian Freedom Fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Lithuanian_Freedom_Fighters), was captured and executed in 1954, his successor as chairman [Adolfas Ramanauskas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolfas_Ramanauskas) was brutally tortured and executed in 1957.[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82] Since the late 1980s [Sąjūdis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%85j%C5%ABdis) movement sought for the restoration of independent Lithuania, and in 1989 the [Baltic Way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Way) was held.[^cite_note-VleListorija-80]&#x20;

### Since 1990

Main article: [Act of the Re-Establishment of the State of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_the_Re-Establishment_of_the_State_of_Lithuania)

See also: [Singing Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singing_Revolution)





On 11 March 1990, the [Supreme Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Council_%E2%80%93_Reconstituent_Seimas) announced the [restoration of Lithuania's independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_the_Re-Establishment_of_the_State_of_Lithuania).

[^cite_note-Bell2002-83]

&#x20;After refusal to revoke the Act, [Soviet forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Ground_Forces) stormed the [Seimas Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seimas_Palace) while Lithuanians defended the democratically elected Council. The Act, the first such declaration in the USSR, later was a model and inspiration to other [Soviet republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_republics), and strongly influenced the [dissolution of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union).

On 11 March 1990, the [Supreme Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Council_%E2%80%93_Reconstituent_Seimas) announced the restoration of Lithuania's independence. Lithuania became the first Soviet-occupied state to announce the restitution of independence.[^cite_note-\:0-84] On 20 April 1990, the Soviets [imposed an economic blockade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_economic_blockade_of_Lithuania) by ceasing to deliver raw materials to Lithuania.[^cite_note-85] Domestic industry and the population started feeling the lack of fuel, essential goods, and even hot water. Although the blockade lasted for 74 days, Lithuania did not renounce its declaration of independence.[^cite_note-LT1990-86]&#x20;

Gradually, economic relations were restored. However, tensions peaked again in January 1991. Attempts were made to carry out a coup d'état using the [Soviet Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Armed_Forces), the Internal Army of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and the USSR Committee for State Security ([KGB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGB)). Because of the poor economic conditions in Lithuania, the authorities in Moscow believed the coup would receive strong public support.[^cite_note-87] However, people flocked to Vilnius to defend the [Supreme Council of the Republic of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Council_%E2%80%93_Reconstituent_Seimas) and independence. The coup ended with limited material losses, though the [Soviet Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Ground_Forces) killed 14 people and injured hundreds. A large part of the Lithuanian population participated in the [January Events](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_Events).[^cite_note-bbc-88][^cite_note-89][^cite_note-LT1990-86] On 31 July 1991, Soviet paramilitaries killed 7 Lithuanian border guards on the Soviet (Byelorussian SSR) border in what became known as the [Medininkai Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medininkai_Massacre).[^cite_note-LT1990-86] On 17 September, Lithuania was admitted to the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-LT1990-86]&#x20;

On 25 October 1992, citizens voted in a referendum to adopt the [current constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Lithuania).[^cite_note-LT1990-86] On 14 February 1993, during the direct general elections, [Algirdas Brazauskas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algirdas_Brazauskas) became the first president after the restoration of independence.[^cite_note-LT1990-86] On 31 August 1993 the last units of the Russian Army left Lithuania.[^cite_note-90]&#x20;

On 31 May 2001, Lithuania joined the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization).[^cite_note-91] Since March 2004, Lithuania has been part of [NATO](21133.md).[^cite_note-92] On 1 May 2004, it became a full member of the [European Union](9317.md),[^cite_note-93] and a member of the [Schengen Agreement](179592.md) in December 2007.[^cite_note-94] On 1 January 2015, [Lithuania joined](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_and_the_euro) the [eurozone](184391.md) and adopted the European Union's single currency.[^cite_note-95] On 4 July 2018, Lithuania officially joined the [OECD](33853117.md).[^cite_note-96] On 24 February 2022, Lithuania declared a state of emergency in response to the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md).[^cite_note-97] Together with seven other NATO member states, it invoked NATO [Article 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty#Article_4) to hold consultations on security.[^cite_note-98]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Lithuania)



Physical map and geomorphological subdivision of Lithuania

Lithuania is located in the Baltic region of [Europe](9239.md)[^cite_note-location-28] and covers an area of 65,300 km<sup>2</sup> (25,200 sq mi).[^cite_note-99] It lies between latitudes [53°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/53rd_parallel_north) and [57° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/57th_parallel_north), and mostly between longitudes [21°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_meridian_east) and [27° E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/27th_meridian_east) (part of the [Curonian Spit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curonian_Spit) lies west of 21°). It has around 99 kilometres (61.5 mi) of sandy coastline, with around 38 kilometres (24 mi) facing the open [Baltic Sea](3335.md). The rest of the coast is sheltered by the Curonian sand peninsula. The warm-water port at [Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da) lies at the narrow mouth of the [Curonian Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curonian_Lagoon) (Lithuanian: *Kuršių marios*), a shallow lagoon extending south to [Kaliningrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaliningrad). The country's main and largest river, the [Nemunas River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemunas_River), and some of its tributaries carry international shipping.&#x20;

Lithuania lies at the edge of the [North European Plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_European_Plain). Its landscape was smoothed by the glaciers of the [last ice age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_glacial_period), and is a combination of moderate lowlands and highlands. Its highest point is [Aukštojas Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auk%C5%A1tojas_Hill) at 294 metres (965 ft) in the eastern part of the country. The terrain features [numerous lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_of_Lithuania) and wetlands, and a mixed forest zone covers over 33% of the country. [Drūkšiai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr%C5%ABk%C5%A1iai) is the largest lake, [Tauragnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tauragnas) is the deepest lake, and [Asveja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asveja) is the longest lake in Lithuania.&#x20;

After a re-estimation of the boundaries of the European continent in 1989, Jean-George Affholder, a scientist at the [Institut Géographique National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institut_g%C3%A9ographique_national_\(France\)) (French National Geographic Institute), determined that the [geographic centre of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_midpoint_of_Europe) was in Lithuania, at [54°54′N 25°19′E / 54.900°N 25.317°E / 54.900; 25.317 (Purnuškės (centre of gravity))](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Lithuania\&params=54_54_N_25_19_E_type\:landmark\&title=Purnu%C5%A1k%C4%97s+%28centre+of+gravity%29), 26 kilometres (16 mi) north of Vilnius.[^cite_note-100] Affholder accomplished this by calculating the [centre of gravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_of_mass) of the [geometrical figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Europe) of Europe.&#x20;

### Climate

Main article: [Geography of Lithuania § Climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Lithuania#Climate)

Lithuania has a temperate climate with both [maritime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate) and [continental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_climate) influences. It is defined as [humid continental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) (Dfb) under the [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) (but is close to oceanic in a narrow coastal zone).&#x20;

Average temperatures on the coast are −2.5 °C (27.5 °F) in January and 16 °C (61 °F) in July. In Vilnius, the average temperatures are −6 °C (21 °F) in January and 17 °C (63 °F) in July. During the summer, 20 °C (68 °F) is common during the day, while 14 °C (57 °F) is common at night; in the past, temperatures have reached as high as 30 or 35 °C (86 or 95 °F). Some winters can be very cold. −20 °C (−4 °F) occurs almost every winter. Winter extremes are −34 °C (−29 °F) in coastal areas and −43 °C (−45 °F) in the east.&#x20;

The average annual precipitation is 800 mm (31.5 in) on the coast, 900 mm (35.4 in) in the Samogitia highlands, and 600 mm (23.6 in) in the east. Snow occurs every year, and it can snow from October to April. In some years, sleet can fall in September or May. The growing season lasts 202 days in the western part of the country and 169 days in the eastern part. Severe storms are rare in the east but common in the coastal areas. Lithuania experienced a drought in 2002, causing forest and [peat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peat) bog fires.[^cite_note-101]&#x20;

### Biodiversity and conservation

Main articles: [Fauna of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna_of_Lithuania), [Flora of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_Lithuania), and [Protected areas of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Lithuania)



Lithuanian flatlands with lakes, swamps and forests. Lithuania has thousands of lakes.



Sand dunes of the [Curonian Spit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curonian_Spit) near [Nida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nida,_Lithuania), which are the highest drifting sand dunes in Europe ([UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md))

[^cite_note-102]

The *Aplinkos apsaugos įstatymas* (Environmental Protection Act) was adopted in 1992. The law provides the foundations for regulating social relations in the field of environmental protection, establishes the basic rights and obligations of legal and natural persons in preserving the biodiversity, ecological systems, and the landscape.[^cite_note-103] Lithuania agreed to cut [carbon emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_emissions) by at least 20% of 1990 levels by 2020 and by at least 40% by 2030, together with all European Union members. Also, by 2020 at least 20% (27% by 2030) of the country's total energy consumption should be from the [renewable energy](25784.md) sources.[^cite_note-104] In 2016, Lithuania introduced especially effective [container deposit legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Container_deposit_legislation), which resulted in collecting 92% of all packagings in 2017.[^cite_note-105]&#x20;

Lithuania does not have high mountains, and its landscape is dominated by blooming meadows, dense forests and fertile fields of cereals. However, it stands out by the abundance of [hillforts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillfort), which previously had castles where the ancient Lithuanians burned altars for pagan gods.[^cite_note-gamta-106] Lithuania is a particularly watered region with more than 3,000 lakes, mostly in the northeast. The country is also drained by numerous rivers, most notably the longest [Nemunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemunas).[^cite_note-gamta-106] Lithuania is home to two terrestrial ecoregions: [Central European mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_European_mixed_forests) and [Sarmatic mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarmatic_mixed_forests).[^cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-107]&#x20;

Forest has long been one of the most important natural resources. Forests occupy one-third of the territory, and timber-related industrial production accounts for almost 11% of industrial production.[^cite_note-108] There are five [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_parks_in_the_Baltics),[^cite_note-109] 30 [regional parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_regional_parks_of_Lithuania),[^cite_note-110] 402 nature reserves,[^cite_note-111] 6 [strict nature reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strict_nature_reserve),[^cite_note-112] and 668 state-protected natural heritage objects.[^cite_note-113]&#x20;



The [white stork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_stork) is the national bird of Lithuania, which has the highest-density stork population in Europe.

[^cite_note-114]

[^cite_note-115]

Ecosystems include natural and semi-natural (forests, [bogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bog), wetlands and meadows) and anthropogenic (agrarian and urban) ecosystems. Among natural ecosystems, forests are particularly important, covering 33% of the country's territory. Wetlands (raised bogs, [fens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fen), transitional mires, etc.) cover 7.9% of the country, with 70% of wetlands having been lost due to drainage and peat extraction between 1960 and 1980. Changes in wetland plant communities resulted in the replacement of moss and grass communities by trees and shrubs, and fens not directly affected by land reclamation have become drier as a result of a drop in the water table. There are 29,000 rivers with a total length of 64,000 km; the Nemunas River basin occupies 74% of the territory of the country. Due to the construction of dams, approximately 70% of spawning sites of potential [catadromous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_migration) fish species have disappeared. In some cases, river and lake ecosystems continue to be impacted by anthropogenic [eutrophication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eutrophication).[^cite_note-cbd-116]&#x20;

Agricultural land comprises 54% of Lithuania's territory (roughly 70% of that is arable land and 30% meadows and pastures), approximately 400,000 ha of agricultural land is not farmed and acts as an ecological niche for weeds and invasive plant species. Habitat deterioration is occurring in regions with very productive and expensive lands as crop areas are expanded. About 18.9% of all plant species, including 1.87% of all known fungi species and 31% of all known species of lichens, are listed in the [Lithuanian Red Data Book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_extinct_and_endangered_species_of_Lithuania). The list also contains 8% of all fish species.[^cite_note-cbd-116]&#x20;

The wildlife populations have rebounded as hunting became more restricted and urbanisation allowed replanting forests (forests already tripled in size since their lows). Lithuania has approximately 250,000 larger wild animals or 5 per square kilometre. The most prolific large wild animal is the [roe deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_deer), with 120,000 of them. They are followed by [boars](52255.md) (55,000). Other ungulates are the [red deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_deer) (\~22,000), [fallow deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fallow_deer) (\~21,000) and the largest one: [moose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moose) (\~7,000). Among the predators, [foxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_fox) are the most common (\~27,000). [Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_wolf), which are ingrained in local mythology more than in reality, number only about 800 in Lithuania. Even rarer are [lynxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_lynx) (\~200). There are about 200,000 rabbits living throughout the country's forests.[^cite_note-117]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main article: [Politics of Lithuania](17823.md)



[Seimas](571739.md), Parliament of Lithuania

### Government

Since Lithuania declared the restoration of its independence on 11 March 1990, it has maintained strong democratic traditions. It held its first independent general elections on 25 October 1992, in which 56.75% of voters supported the [new constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Lithuania).[^cite_note-referenda-118] There were intense debates concerning the constitution, particularly the role of the president. A [separate referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Lithuanian_presidency_referendum) was held on 23 May 1992 to gauge public opinion on the matter, and 41% of voters supported the restoration of the [President of Lithuania](273654.md).[^cite_note-referenda-118] Through compromise, a [semi-presidential system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-presidential_republic) was agreed on.[^cite_note-Lina-4]&#x20;



[Gitanas Nausėda](58519561.md)\
[President](273654.md)



[Mindaugas Sinkevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindaugas_Sinkevi%C4%8Dius)\
[Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Lithuania)



[Juozas Olekas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juozas_Olekas)\
[Speaker of the Seimas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_Seimas)

The Lithuanian [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) is the president, directly elected for a five-year term and serving a maximum of two terms. The president oversees foreign affairs and national security and is the [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the military.[^cite_note-lrpf-119] The president appoints the [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Lithuania) and the cabinet, as well as other top civil servants and the judges for all courts except the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Lithuania).[^cite_note-lrpf-119] President [Gitanas Nausėda](58519561.md) was [elected on 26 May 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Lithuanian_presidential_election) by winning in all the [municipalities of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Lithuania) in the second election round.[^cite_note-president2019-120] He was re-elected in 2024, winning more than 74% of the run-off votes.[^cite_note-121]&#x20;

The judges of the Constitutional Court (*Konstitucinis Teismas*) serve nine-year terms. One-third of the court members is renewed every three years. The judges are appointed by the [Seimas](571739.md) on the nomination by the president, chairman of the Seimas, and the chairman of the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Lithuania). The unicameral Lithuanian parliament, the Seimas, has 141 members elected to four-year terms: 71 in single-member constituencies, and the others in a nationwide vote by [proportional representation](23298.md). A party must receive at least 5% of the national vote to be eligible for any of the 70 national seats in the Seimas.[^cite_note-Seimas-122]&#x20;

According to [International IDEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Democracy_and_Electoral_Assistance)'s Global State of Democracy (GSoD) Indices and Democracy Tracker, Lithuania performs in the mid to high range on overall democratic measures, with particular weaknesses in civic engagement and electoral participation.[^cite_note-123]&#x20;

### Political parties and elections

Main article: [Elections in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Lithuania)



Commemoration of the [Act of the Re-Establishment of the State of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_the_Re-Establishment_of_the_State_of_Lithuania) in the historical [Seimas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seimas_Palace) hall where it was originally signed in 1990. The ceremony is attended by the Lithuanian President, Prime Minister, Chairman of the Seimas and other high-ranking officials.

Lithuania exhibits a fragmented multi-party system,[^cite_note-124] with many small parties in which coalition governments are common. Elections for president take place on the last Sunday no more than two months before the end of current presidential term.[^cite_note-Constitution-125] Ordinary elections to the Seimas take place on the second Sunday of October every four years.[^cite_note-Seimas-122] The [Social Democratic Party of Lithuania](425771.md) won the [2024 Lithuanian parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lithuanian_parliamentary_election) and gained 52 of 141 seats in the parliament.[^cite_note-126] In November 2024, [Gintautas Paluckas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gintautas_Paluckas) was confirmed as the prime minister after the Social Democrats reached a coalition agreement with [Union of Democrats "For Lithuania"](70139653.md) and [Dawn of Nemunas](75998749.md).[^cite_note-127] Lithuania was one of the first countries in the world to [grant women a right to vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_suffrage) in the elections. Women were allowed to vote by the [1918 Constitution of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Lithuania#Interwar_constitutions) and used their newly granted right for the first time in 1919.[^cite_note-128]&#x20;

Each municipality is governed by a municipal council and a mayor, who is a member of the municipal council. The number of members, elected on a four-year term, in each municipal council depends on the size of the municipality and varies from 15 (in municipalities with fewer than 5,000 residents) to 51 (in municipalities with more than 500,000 residents). Members of the council, with the exception of the mayor, are elected using proportional representation. Starting with 2015, the mayor is elected directly by the majority of residents of the municipality.[^cite_note-129] The Social Democratic Party of Lithuania won the most positions in the 2023 elections (358 municipal council seats and 17 mayors).[^cite_note-130]&#x20;

As of 2024, the number of seats in the [European Parliament](9581.md) allocated to Lithuania was 11.[^cite_note-131] Ordinary elections take place on a Sunday on the same day as in other EU countries. Eight political parties gained seats in the [2024 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election_in_Lithuania).[^cite_note-132]&#x20;

### Law and law enforcement

Main article: [Law of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Lithuania)



[Statutes of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutes_of_Lithuania) were the central piece of Lithuanian law in 1529–1795.

The first attempt to codify the Lithuanian laws was in 1468 when the [Casimir's Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casimir%27s_Code) was compiled and adopted by Grand Duke [Casimir IV Jagiellon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casimir_IV_Jagiellon).[^cite_note-LawVle-133] In the 16th century three editions of the [Statutes of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutes_of_Lithuania) were created with the First Statute being adopted in 1529, the Second Statute in 1566, and the Third Statute in 1588.[^cite_note-LawVle-133] On 3 May 1791, the Europe's first and the world's second [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_3_May_1791) was adopted by the [Great Sejm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Sejm).[^cite_note-134] The Third Statute was partly in force in the territory of Lithuania even until 1840, despite the [Third Partition of the Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partitions_of_Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth) in 1795.[^cite_note-LawVle-133] Internal state acts, international correspondence and resolutions of the [Sejm of the Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sejm_of_the_Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth) had to be confirmed with the [Great Seal of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Seal_of_Lithuania) or the [Lesser Seal of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesser_Seal_of_Lithuania) to come into force in Lithuania until 1795.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-KanclerisVle-136]&#x20;

In 1934–1935, Lithuania held the [first mass trial of the Nazis in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Neumann_and_Sass); the convicted were sentenced to imprisonment in a heavy labor prison and capital punishments.[^cite_note-137]&#x20;

After regaining of independence in 1990, the largely modified Soviet legal codes were in force for about a decade. The current Constitution of Lithuania was adopted on 25 October 1992.[^cite_note-138] In 2001, the [Civil Code of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Code_of_Lithuania) was passed in Seimas. It was succeeded by the Criminal Code and Criminal Procedure Code in 2003. The approach to the criminal law is inquisitorial, as opposed to adversarial; it is generally characterised by an insistence on formality and rationalisation, as opposed to practicality and informality. Normative legal act enters into force on the next day after its publication in the *Teisės aktų registras*, unless it has a later entry into force date.[^cite_note-139] The [European Union law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_law) is an integral part of the Lithuanian legal system since 1 May 2004.[^cite_note-140]&#x20;

After breaking away from the Soviet Union, Lithuiana had a difficult crime situation, however, the law enforcement agencies fought crime over the years, making Lithuania a reasonably safe country.[^cite_note-141] Crime in Lithuania has been declining rapidly.[^cite_note-142] Law enforcement is primarily the responsibility of local *Lietuvos policija* (Lithuanian Police) commissariats. They are supplemented by the *Lietuvos policijos antiteroristinių operacijų rinktinė Aras* (Anti-Terrorist Operations Team of the Lithuanian Police *Aras*), *Lietuvos kriminalinės policijos biuras* (Lithuanian Criminal Police Bureau), *Lietuvos policijos kriminalistinių tyrimų centras* (Lithuanian Police Forensic Research Center) and *Lietuvos kelių policijos tarnyba* (Lithuanian Road Police Service).[^cite_note-143]&#x20;

In 2017, there were 63,846 crimes registered in Lithuania. Of these, thefts comprised a large part with 19,630 cases (13.2% less than in 2016). While 2,835 crimes were serious and very serious (crimes that may lead to more than six years imprisonment), which is 14.5% less than in 2016. In total, 129 homicides or attempted homicide occurred (19.9% less than in 2016), while serious bodily harm was registered 178 times (17.6% less than in 2016). Another problematic crime [contraband](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contraband) cases also decreased by 27.2% from 2016. Meanwhile, crimes in electronic data and information technology security fields increased by 26.6%.[^cite_note-144] In the 2024 Special Eurobarometer, 24% of Lithuanians said that corruption affects their daily lives (EU average 27%). Moreover, 83% of Lithuanians regarded corruption as widespread in their country (EU average 68%), and 78% agreed that bribery and the use of connections is often the easiest way of obtaining certain public services (EU average 63%).[^cite_note-145]&#x20;

[Capital punishment in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_Lithuania) was suspended in 1996 and eliminated in 1998.[^cite_note-146] Imprisonment rate in Lithuania is among the highest in the EU, although it has decreased by nearly half between 2013 and 2023.[^cite_note-147] According to scientist Gintautas Sakalauskas, the high imprisonment rate is not because of a high criminality rate in the country, but due to Lithuania's high repression level and mistrust in the society.[^cite_note-148]&#x20;

Cultural regions of Lithuania divided by [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Lithuania) and [elderships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elderships_of_Lithuania):\


&#x20; [Lithuania Minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_Minor) (Mažoji Lietuva)

&#x20; [Samogitia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samogitia) (Žemaitija)

&#x20; [Highland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auk%C5%A1taitija) (Aukštaitija)

&#x20; [Dainava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dz%C5%ABkija) (Dzūkija)

&#x20; [Sudovia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suvalkija) (Suvalkija)

The current system of administrative division was established in 1994 and modified in 2000 to meet the requirements of the European Union. The country's [10 counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: singular – *apskritis*, plural – *apskritys*) are subdivided into [60 municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: singular – *savivaldybė*, plural – *savivaldybės*), and further divided into 546 [elderships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elderships_of_Lithuania) (Lithuanian: singular – *seniūnija*, plural – *seniūnijos*). There are also 5 distinct [cultural regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_regions_of_Lithuania) – [Dzūkija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dz%C5%ABkija), [Aukštaitija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auk%C5%A1taitija), [Suvalkija](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suvalkija), [Samogitia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samogitia) and [Lithuania Minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_Minor).&#x20;

Municipalities have been the most important unit of administration since the system of county governorship (*apskrities viršininkas*) was dissolved in 2010.[^cite_note-county-149] Some municipalities are historically called "district municipalities" (often shortened to "district"), while others are called "city municipalities" (sometimes shortened to "city"). Each has its own elected government. The election of municipality councils originally occurred every three years, but now takes place every four years. The council appoints [elders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elder_\(administrative_title\)) to govern the elderships. Mayors have been directly elected since 2015; prior to that, they were appointed by the council.[^cite_note-150]&#x20;

Elderships are the smallest administrative units and do not play a role in national politics. They provide necessary local public services—for example, registering births and deaths in rural areas. They are most active in the social sector, identifying needy individuals or families and organising and distributing welfare and other forms of relief.[^cite_note-elderates-151] Some citizens feel that elderships have no real power and receive too little attention, and that they could otherwise become a source of local initiative for addressing rural problems.[^cite_note-152]&#x20;
