### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Lithuania)

Lithuania became a member of the United Nations on 18 September 1991, and is a signatory to a number of its organisations and other international agreements. It is also a member of the [European Union](9317.md), the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), [OSCE](22796.md), as well as [NATO](21133.md) and its adjunct North Atlantic Coordinating Council. Lithuania gained membership in the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) on 31 May 2001, and joined the [OECD](33853117.md) on 5 July 2018,[^cite_note-Lithuania's_accession_to_the_OECD-155] while also seeking membership in other Western organizations. Lithuania has established diplomatic relations with 149 countries.[^cite_note-156] During the second half of 2013, Lithuania assumed the role of the [presidency of the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_the_Council_of_the_European_Union). Lithuania is active in developing cooperation among northern European countries. It is a member of the interparliamentary [Baltic Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Assembly), the intergovernmental [Baltic Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Council_of_Ministers) and the [Council of the Baltic Sea States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_the_Baltic_Sea_States).&#x20;



Stamp dedicated to Lithuania's presidency of the European Union

Lithuania cooperates with Nordic and the two other Baltic countries through the [Nordic-Baltic Eight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic-Baltic_Eight) format. A similar format, NB6, unites Nordic and Baltic members of EU. NB6's focus is to discuss and agree on positions before presenting them to the Council of the European Union and at the meetings of EU foreign affairs ministers. The [Council of the Baltic Sea States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_the_Baltic_Sea_States) (CBSS) was established in [Copenhagen](5166.md) in 1992 as an informal regional political forum. Its main aim is to promote integration and to close contacts between the region's countries. The members of CBSS are [Iceland](14531.md), [Sweden](5058739.md), [Denmark](76972.md), [Norway](21241.md), [Finland](10577.md), [Germany](11867.md), Lithuania, [Latvia](17514.md), [Estonia](28222445.md), [Poland](22936.md), [Russia](25391.md), and the European Commission. Its observer states are [Belarus](3457.md), [France](5843419.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Netherlands](21148.md), [Romania](25445.md), [Slovakia](26830.md), [Spain](26667.md), the [United States](3434750.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md), and [Ukraine](31750.md).&#x20;

The [Nordic Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Council) and Lithuania engage in political cooperation to attain mutual goals and to determine new trends and possibilities for joint cooperation. The council's information office aims to disseminate [Nordic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries) concepts and to demonstrate and promote Nordic cooperation. Together with the five Nordic countries and the two other Baltic countries, it is a member of the [Nordic Investment Bank (NIB)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Investment_Bank) and cooperates in its NORDPLUS programme, which is committed to education.&#x20;



Lithuania was a member of the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md). Its representatives are on the right side.

Poland was highly supportive of Lithuanian independence, despite the [discriminatory treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanization) of its [Polish minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poles_in_Lithuania).[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] The former [Solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_\(Polish_trade_union\)) leader and Polish President [Lech Wałęsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lech_Wa%C5%82%C4%99sa) criticised the government of Lithuania over discrimination against the Polish minority and rejected the [Order of Vytautas the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Vytautas_the_Great).[^cite_note-159] Lithuania maintains warm relations with [Georgia](48768.md) and strongly supports its European Union and NATO aspirations.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162] During the [Russo-Georgian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War) in 2008, when the Russian troops were occupying the territory of Georgia and approaching towards the Georgian capital [Tbilisi](42298.md), President [Valdas Adamkus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valdas_Adamkus), together with the Polish and Ukrainian presidents, went to Tbilisi by answering to the Georgians request of the international assistance.[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] Shortly, Lithuanians and the [Lithuanian Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Lithuania) also began collecting financial support for the war victims.[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166]&#x20;

In 2004–2009, [Dalia Grybauskaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalia_Grybauskait%C4%97) served as [European Commissioner for Financial Programming and the Budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Commissioner_for_Financial_Programming_and_the_Budget) within the [José Manuel Barroso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Manuel_Barroso)-led Commission.[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168]&#x20;



Guests of the 2023 Vilnius (NATO) summit in the Courtyard of the Presidential Palace in Vilnius

In 2013, Lithuania was elected to the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) for a two-year term,[^cite_note-169] becoming the first Baltic country elected to this post. During its membership, Lithuania actively supported [Ukraine](31750.md) and often condemned Russia for the [war in Ukraine](42085878.md), immediately earning Ukrainian esteem.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] As the [war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas) progressed, President Dalia Grybauskaitė compared the Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) to [Josef Stalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josef_Stalin) and to [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md); she also called Russia a "terrorist state".[^cite_note-172]&#x20;

In 2018 Lithuania, Latvia, and Estonia were awarded the [Peace of Westphalia Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Peace_of_Westphalia_Prize\&action=edit\&redlink=1) for their exceptional model of democratic development and contribution to peace in the continent.[^cite_note-173] In 2019 Lithuania condemned the [Turkish offensive into north-eastern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Turkish_offensive_into_north-eastern_Syria).[^cite_note-174] In December 2021, Lithuania reported that in an escalation of the diplomatic spat with China over its [relations with Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania%E2%80%93Taiwan_relations),[^cite_note-175] China had stopped all imports from Lithuania.[^cite_note-176] According to Lithuanian intelligence agencies, in 2023 there was an increase in Chinese intelligence activity against Lithuania, including cyberespionage and increased focus on Lithuania's internal affairs and foreign policy.[^cite_note-177]&#x20;

The 2023 [NATO summit](11190132.md) was held in Vilnius.[^cite_note-178]&#x20;

### Military



Lithuanian Army soldiers with their [NATO](21133.md) allies during Iron Sword 2014



Lithuanian [Panzerhaubitze 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panzerhaubitze_2000) firing during an exercise in 2022

The [Lithuanian Armed Forces](17827.md) comprises the [Lithuanian Land Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Land_Force), [Lithuanian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Air_Force), [Lithuanian Naval Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Naval_Force), [Lithuanian Special Operations Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Special_Operations_Force) and other units: Logistics Command, Training and Doctrine Command, Headquarters Battalion, [Military Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Military_Police). Directly subordinated to the [Chief of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Defence_\(Lithuania\)) are the Special Operations Forces and Military Police. The Reserve Forces are under command of the [Lithuanian National Defence Volunteer Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_National_Defence_Volunteer_Forces).&#x20;

The Lithuanian Armed Forces consist of some 20,000 active personnel, which may be supported by reserve forces.[^cite_note-179] Compulsory conscription ended in 2008 but was reintroduced in 2015.[^cite_note-180] As of 2024, the Lithuanian Armed Forces have 30 soldiers and officers participating in nine international operations and European Union training missions deployed in [Kosovo](17391.md), [Iraq](7515928.md), [Central African Republic](5478.md), [Djibouti](17207794.md), [Mozambique](19301.md), [Spain](26667.md), [Italy](14532.md), and in the [United Kingdom](31717.md), providing training for Ukrainian soldiers on [Operation Interflex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Interflex).[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182]&#x20;

Lithuania became a full member of [NATO](21133.md) in March 2004. Fighter jets of NATO members are deployed in [Šiauliai Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iauliai_Air_Base) and [provide security for the Baltic airspace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Air_Policing). Since 2014, Lithuania participates in the British-led [Joint Expeditionary Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Expeditionary_Force).[^cite_note-183]&#x20;

Beginning in summer of 2005, Lithuania was part of the [International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force) in Afghanistan, leading a [Provincial Reconstruction Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_Reconstruction_Team) in [Chaghcharan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaghcharan) in [Ghor Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghor_Province). Since joining international operations in 1994, Lithuania has lost two soldiers: Lieutenant Normundas Valteris fell in Bosnia, as his patrol vehicle drove over a mine. Sergeant Arūnas Jarmalavičius was fatally wounded during an attack on the camp of his Provincial Reconstruction Team in Afghanistan.[^cite_note-184]&#x20;

The Lithuanian National Defence Policy aims to guarantee the preservation of the independence and sovereignty of the state, the integrity of its land, territorial waters and airspace, and its constitutional order. Its main strategic goals are to defend the country's interests, and to maintain and expand the capabilities of its armed forces so they may contribute to and participate in the missions of NATO and European Union member states.[^cite_note-WB-185]&#x20;

The [defense ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defence_\(Lithuania\)) is responsible for combat forces, search and rescue, and intelligence operations. The 5,000 [border guards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_State_Border_Guard_Service) fall under the Interior Ministry's supervision and are responsible for border protection, passport and customs duties, and share responsibility with the navy for smuggling and drug trafficking interdiction. A special security department handles VIP protection and communications security. In 2015 [National Cyber Security Centre of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Cyber_Security_Centre_of_Lithuania) was created. Paramilitary organisation [Lithuanian Riflemen's Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Riflemen%27s_Union) acts as a civilian self-defence institution.&#x20;

According to NATO, in 2020, Lithuania allocated 2.13% of its [GDP](12594.md) to the national defense.[^cite_note-nato-lt-spending-186] For a long time, especially after the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis), Lithuania lagged behind NATO allies in terms of defence spending. However, it increased funding, exceeding the NATO guideline of 2% in 2019. President Nausėda called for more NATO troops on 22 April 2022, saying NATO should increase its deployment of troops in Lithuania and elsewhere on Europe's eastern flank following the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), during a meeting in Vilnius.[^cite_note-187]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Lithuania)

See also: [List of companies of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_of_Lithuania)



Real GDP per capita development of Estonia, Latvia and Lithuania



Lithuanian counties by GDP per capita, 2022

[^cite_note-Counties-154]



Comparison of Lithuania's GDP per capita to rest of the world where countries with higher GDP per capita are marked in red (2022)

[^cite_note-188]

Lithuania has an open and [mixed economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy) that is classified as a [high-income economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-income_economy) by the [World Bank](36752.md).[^cite_note-189] As of 2017 the three largest sectors were services (63% of GDP), industry (24%) and agriculture (3%).[^cite_note-190] On 1 January 2015, the euro became the national currency, replacing [litas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_litas), which had been in circulation since 1993.[^cite_note-iso4217news2014-191]&#x20;

Mineral products comprised the largest share of exports (14%) in 2024; other major sectors include machinery and appliances, electrical equipment (14%), chemical products (11%), food, beverages and tobacco products (9%). The largest markets for exports were Latvia (12%), Poland (10%), and Germany (9%).[^cite_note-192] Exports equaled 88% of GDP in 2022.[^cite_note-193]&#x20;

GDP experienced very high real growth rates for the decade up to 2009, peaking at 11% in 2007. As a result, the country was often termed a [Baltic Tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_Tiger). However, in 2009 due to the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis), GDP contracted 15%[^cite_note-194] and unemployment rate reached 17.8% in 2010.[^cite_note-195] Growth has since been much slower. According to the IMF, financial conditions are conducive to growth and financial soundness indicators remain strong. The public debt ratio in 2016 was 40% of GDP; it had been 15% in 2008.[^cite_note-196]&#x20;

On average, more than 95% of all [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment) (FDI) comes from EU countries. Sweden is historically the largest investor with 20% – 30% of FDI.[^cite_note-197] FDI into Lithuania spiked in 2017, reaching its highest ever recorded number of greenfield investment projects. In 2017, Lithuania was third, after [Ireland](147575.md) and [Singapore](27318.md) by the average job value of investment projects.[^cite_note-198] The US was the leading source country in 2017, 25% of total FDI.[^cite_note-199] Based on the Eurostat's data, in 2017 the value of exports recorded the most rapid growth of Baltic states and across Europe at 17%.[^cite_note-200]&#x20;

Between 2004 and 2016, one of five Lithuanians emigrated, seeking better opportunities and higher salaries abroad.[^cite_note-201] Long term emigration and economic growth has resulted in a shortage in the labor market[^cite_note-202] and growth in salaries being larger than growth in labor efficiency.[^cite_note-archive_org_11-203] Unemployment in 2017 was 8%.[^cite_note-204] As of 2022, median wealth per adult was [\$](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USD)32,000 (mean was \$70,000), while total national wealth was \$147 billion.[^cite_note-CS2023-1-205] As of 2023 Q2, the average monthly gross salary was €2,000.\[^cite_note-[https://osp.stat.gov.lt-206](https://osp.stat.gov.lt-206/)] Implicit tax rates on labor, capital and corporate income in Lithuania have been among of the lowest in the EU.[^cite_note-epp.eurostat.ec.europa.eu-207] The nominal personal income tax rate is 20%, with 32% applied on high incomes. The corporate tax rate is 16% for most companies and 6% for small businesses; 7 [free economic zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_economic_zone) operate.[^cite_note-208]&#x20;

[Information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology) production is growing, reaching €2 billion in 2016.[^cite_note-209] In 2017, 35[^cite_note-210] [financial technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_technology) companies came to Lithuania as a result of the government and [Bank of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Lithuania) simplifying procedures.[^cite_note-211] Lithuania has granted a total of 39 e-money licenses, second in the EU to the UK. In 2018, [Google](1092923.md) set up a payment company in Lithuania.[^cite_note-212] Europe's first international [blockchain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockchain) centre launched in Vilnius in 2018.[^cite_note-213] Since 2021, Lithuania has issued hundreds of licenses for cryptocurrency exchange and storage operations, making it one of the leading countries in the EU in this sector.[^cite_note-214]&#x20;

### Agriculture

Main article: [Agriculture in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Lithuania)

Agriculture has been one of Lithuania's most important occupations for many centuries.[^cite_note-215] Accession to the European Union in 2004 ushered in a new agricultural era. The EU pursues a very high standard of food safety and purity. In 1999, the Seimas adopted a law on product safety, and in 2000 it adopted a law on food.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217] The reform of the agricultural market has been carried out on the basis of these two laws.&#x20;

In 2016, agricultural production was €2.3 billion. [Cereal crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cereal_crops) occupied the largest part (5710 tons); other significant types include: [sugar beet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_beet) (934 tons), [rapeseed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapeseed) (393 tons) and [potatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potato) (340 tons). Products totaling €4,385 million were exported to foreign markets, of which products for €3,165 million were of Lithuanian origin. Export of agricultural and food products accounted for 19% of all exports of goods.[^cite_note-218]&#x20;

[Organic farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_farming) is becoming more popular. The status of organic growers and producers is granted by the public body *Ekoagros*. In 2016, there were 2539 such farms that occupied 225,542 hectares. Of these, 43% were cereals, 31% perennial grasses, 14% leguminous crops and 12% others.[^cite_note-219]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Lithuania ranks moderately in the [International Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Innovation_Index),[^cite_note-220] and is placed 15th among EU countries by the [European Innovation Scoreboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Innovation_Scoreboard).[^cite_note-221] Lithuania was ranked 33rd in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] Lasers and [biotechnology](4502.md) are flagship fields of the science and high-tech industry.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225] *Šviesos konversija* ("Light Conversion") has developed a [femtosecond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Femtosecond) laser system that has 80% market share worldwide, with applications in DNA research, ophthalmological surgeries, and nanotechnology.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227] The [Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University) Laser Research Center has developed one of the most powerful femtosecond lasers in the world dedicated primarily to [oncological diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oncology).[^cite_note-228] In 1963, [Vytautas Straižys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Strai%C5%BEys) and his colleagues created [Vilnius photometric system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_photometric_system) that is used in astronomy.[^cite_note-229] Noninvasive intracranial pressure and blood flow measuring devices were developed by [Kaunas University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_University_of_Technology) scientist A. Ragauskas.[^cite_note-230] Kęstutis Pyragas contributed to the study of [chaos theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_of_chaos) with his method of delayed feedback control, the [Pyragas method](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyragas_method). [Kavli Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavli_Prize) laureate [Virginijus Šikšnys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginijus_%C5%A0ik%C5%A1nys) is known for his discoveries in [CRISPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CRISPR), namely with respect to CRISPR-[Cas9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cas9).[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]&#x20;

Lithuania has launched three satellites to space: [LitSat-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LitSat-1), [Lituanica SAT-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lituanica_SAT-1) and [LituanicaSAT-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LituanicaSAT-2).[^cite_note-233] [Lithuanian Museum of Ethnocosmology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Museum_of_Ethnocosmology) and [Molėtai Astronomical Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mol%C4%97tai_Astronomical_Observatory) is located in [Kulionys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kulionys).[^cite_note-234] Fifteen R\&D institutions are members of [Lithuanian Space Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Space_Association); Lithuania is a cooperating state with [European Space Agency](10363.md).[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236] [Rimantas Stankevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rimantas_Stankevi%C4%8Dius) is the only ethnically Lithuanian astronaut.[^cite_note-237]&#x20;

Lithuania in 2018 became an Associated Member State of [CERN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CERN).[^cite_note-238] Two CERN incubators in Vilnius and Kaunas will be hosted.[^cite_note-239] The most advanced scientific research is being conducted at the Life Sciences Center,[^cite_note-240] Center For Physical Sciences and Technology.[^cite_note-241] From 2011 to 2016, yearly growth of the biotech and life science sector reached 22%. 16 academic institutions, 15 R\&D centres (science parks and innovation valleys) and more than 370 manufacturers operate in the life science and biotech industry.[^cite_note-242]&#x20;

In 2008 the Valley development programme was started aiming to upgrade scientific research infrastructure and encourage business and science cooperation. Five R\&D Valleys were launched – Jūrinis (maritime technologies), Nemunas (agro, bioenergy, forestry), Saulėtekis (laser and light, semiconductor), Santara (biotechnology, medicine), Santaka (sustainable chemistry and pharmacy).[^cite_note-243] Lithuanian Innovation Center was created to provide support for innovations and research institutions.[^cite_note-244]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Lithuania)



[Druskininkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druskininkai) is a popular [spa town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa_town).

Statistics from 2023 showed 1.4 million tourists from foreign countries visited Lithuania and spent at least one night. The largest number of tourists came from [Poland](22936.md) (173,500), [Latvia](17514.md) (144,300), [Belarus](3457.md) (141,900), [Germany](11867.md) (127,400), the [United Kingdom](31717.md) (74,200), the [United States](3434750.md) (69,700), [Ukraine](31750.md) (67,000), and [Estonia](28222445.md) (61,300).[^cite_note-tou-245]&#x20;

Domestic tourism has been on the rise as well. Currently there are up to 1000 places of attraction in Lithuania. Most tourists visit the big cities—[Vilnius](32597.md), [Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da), and [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas), seaside resorts, such as [Neringa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neringa_Municipality), [Palanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palanga), and [Spa towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa_town) – [Druskininkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druskininkai), [Birštonas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bir%C5%A1tonas).[^cite_note-246]&#x20;

Hot air ballooning is popular, especially in Vilnius and Trakai. Bicycle tourism is growing, especially the Lithuanian Seaside Cycle Route. [EuroVelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroVelo) routes EV10, EV11, EV13 go through Lithuania. The total length of bicycle tracks amounts to 3769 km (of which 1988 km is asphalt pavement).[^cite_note-247] [Nemunas Delta Regional Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemunas_Delta_Regional_Park) and [Žuvintas biosphere reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDuvintas) are known for birdwatching.[^cite_note-248]&#x20;

The total contribution of tourism to GDP had been forecast to rise to €3.2 billion, 7% of GDP by 2027,[^cite_note-249] but has decreased to €1.7 billion, 2.3% of GDP in 2023, although it is rising post [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-MCTo-250]&#x20;

## Communication

Main article: [Telecommunications in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Lithuania)

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(August 2025)* |
| --- | --- |



[Telia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telia_Company) (skyscraper with the old [Teo LT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teo_LT) logo) and [Huawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huawei) headquarters in [Vilnius](32597.md)

Lithuania has a well developed communications infrastructure. The country has 2.8 million citizens[^cite_note-251] and 5 million SIM cards.[^cite_note-252] The largest LTE (4G) mobile network covers 97% of Lithuania's territory.[^cite_note-253] Usage of fixed phone lines has been rapidly decreasing due to rapid expansion of mobile-cellular services.[^cite_note-254] In 2017, Lithuania was top 30 in the world by average mobile broadband speeds and top 20 by average fixed broadband speeds.[^cite_note-255] Lithuania was also top 7 in 2017 in the [List of countries by 4G LTE penetration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_4G_LTE_penetration). In 2016, Lithuania was ranked 17th in United Nations' e-participation index.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] There are four TIER III datacenters in Lithuania.[^cite_note-258] Lithuania is 44th globally ranked country on data center density according to Cloudscene.[^cite_note-259]&#x20;

Development of Rural Areas Broadband Network (RAIN) was started with the objective to provide residents, state and municipal authorities and businesses with fibre-optic broadband access in rural areas. RAIN infrastructure allows 51 communications operators to provide network services to their clients. The project was funded by the European Union and the Lithuanian government.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] 72% of households have access to internet, a number which in 2017 was among EU's lowest[^cite_note-262] and in 2016 ranked 97th by [CIA World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook).[^cite_note-263] The number of households with internet access is expected to increase and reach 77% by 2021.[^cite_note-264] Almost 50% of Lithuanians had smartphones in 2016, a number that is expected to increase to 65% by 2022.[^cite_note-265] Lithuania has the highest FTTH (Fiber to the home) penetration rate in Europe (36.8% in September 2016) according to FTTH Council Europe.[^cite_note-266]&#x20;

## Infrastructure

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Lithuania)



Major highways in Lithuania

[Rail transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Lithuania) consists of 1,762 km (1,095 mi) of 1,520 mm (4 ft 11.8 in) [Russian gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_gauge) railway, of which 122 km (76 mi) are electrified, and 115 km (71 mi) of European [standard gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard-gauge_railway) lines starting at the [Lithuania–Poland border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania%E2%80%93Poland_border).[^cite_note-267] Lithuania received its first railway connection in the middle of the 19th century, when the [Saint Petersburg–Warsaw railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg%E2%80%93Warsaw_railway) was constructed. It included a stretch from [Daugavpils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daugavpils) via Vilnius and Kaunas to Virbalis. The first and only still operating [Kaunas tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_Railway_Tunnel) was completed in 1860. The [Rail Baltica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_Baltica) railway will link Kaunas to Finland, Estonia, Latvia, Poland, and Germany.&#x20;



[Marijampolė railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marijampol%C4%97_railway_station), completed in 1924

Among EU countries, Lithuania has the highest share of freight transported by rail, at 31.7% in 2023.[^cite_note-268] This has dropped over 50% since 2018, caused mainly by [sanctions imposed on Belarusian exports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Belarus#Sanctions) and [Russia](70323386.md).[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] In 2017, [Lithuanian Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Railways) (*Lietuvos Geležinkeliai*), the company that operates most of the railway lines, received an EU penalty for breaching the EU's antitrust laws and restricting competition in rail freight.[^cite_note-271]&#x20;

Lithuania has an extensive network of motorways. WEF grades Lithuanian roads at 4.7 / 7.0[^cite_note-272] and the Lithuanian road authority (LAKD) at 6.5 / 10.0.[^cite_note-273] Lithuanian trucking companies drew attention in 2016[^cite_note-274] and 2017[^cite_note-275] with huge and record-breaking orders of trucks. Almost 90% of commercial truck traffic is international transport, the highest of any EU country.[^cite_note-276] Transportation is the third largest sector of the Lithuanian economy.[^cite_note-277] The sector accounts for 40% of national energy consumption and 75% of oil usage. The car fleet is among the oldest within the European Union and constitutes the most significant single source of domestic [greenhouse gas emissions](9528025.md).[^cite_note-278]&#x20;

The [Port of Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Klaip%C4%97da) is the only commercial cargo port in Lithuania. In 2011, 45.5 million tons of cargo were handled (including [Būtingė oil terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C5%ABting%C4%97_oil_terminal) figures).[^cite_note-279] The port is not among the EU's 20 largest ports,[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281] but it is the eighth largest port in the Baltic Sea region[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] with ongoing expansion plans.[^cite_note-284] As of 2022, the LIWA (Lithuanian Inland Waterways Authority, *Vidaus vandens keliu direkcija* in Lithuanian) is developing a strategy to resurrect cargo shipping on the Nemunas. Its fleet of electric ships will travel 260 km between the Port of Klaipda and the industrial and transportation centre of Kaunas.[^cite_note-\:159-285] The project is anticipated to need a €75.7 million initial investment, and estimated to eliminate 48,000 truck trips annually.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] The inland river cargo port in Marvelė, linking Kaunas and Klaipėda, received its first cargo in 2019.[^cite_note-288]&#x20;

[Vilnius Airport](166489.md) is the largest airport in Lithuania and 91st [busiest airport in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_Europe). It served 3.8 million passengers in 2016.[^cite_note-289] Other airports include [Kaunas Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_Airport), [Palanga International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palanga_International_Airport) and [Šiauliai Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iauliai_International_Airport). Kaunas Airport is a small commercial cargo airport that started regular commercial cargo traffic in 2011.[^cite_note-290]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Lithuania)

See also: [Renewable energy in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Lithuania)



[FSRU *Independence*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FSRU_Independence) in port of Klaipėda

Systematic diversification of energy imports and resources is the key energy strategy.[^cite_note-291] Long-term aims were defined in National Energy Independence strategy in 2012.[^cite_note-292] It was estimated that strategic energy independence initiatives will cost €6.3–7.8 billion and provide annual savings of €0.9–1.1 billion.&#x20;

After the decommissioning of the [Ignalina Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignalina_Nuclear_Power_Plant), Lithuania turned from electricity exporter to electricity importer. As of 2015, 66% of electrical power was imported.[^cite_note-293] Unit No. 1 was closed in 2004, as a condition of entry into the European Union; Unit No. 2 was closed down in 2009. Proposals have been made to construct a new [Visaginas Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visaginas_Nuclear_Power_Plant).[^cite_note-294] However, [a non-binding referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Lithuanian_nuclear_power_referendum) held in 2012 clouded the prospects for the Visaginas project, as 63% of voters said no to a new nuclear power plant.[^cite_note-295]&#x20;



[Kruonis Pumped Storage Plant](1917033.md)

The main source of electrical power is the [Elektrėnai Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elektr%C4%97nai_Power_Plant). Other primary sources are the [Kruonis Pumped Storage Plant](1917033.md) and [Kaunas Hydroelectric Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_Hydroelectric_Power_Plant). Kruonis Pumped Storage Plant is the only power plant in the Baltic states to be used for regulation of the power system's operation with generating capacity of 900 MW for at least 12 hours.[^cite_note-296] First geothermal heating plant ([Klaipėda Geothermal Demonstration Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da_Geothermal_Demonstration_Plant)) in the Baltic Sea region was built in 2004.&#x20;

Lithuania–Sweden submarine electricity interconnection [NordBalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NordBalt) and Lithuania–Poland electricity interconnection [LitPol Link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LitPol_Link) were launched at the end of 2015.[^cite_note-297] In 2018, synchronising the Baltic states' electricity grid with the [Synchronous grid of Continental Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchronous_grid_of_Continental_Europe) has started.[^cite_note-298] In 2016, 20.8% of electricity consumed in Lithuania came from renewable sources.[^cite_note-299]&#x20;

In order to break down [Gazprom](320489.md)'s natural gas monopoly[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] the first large scale LNG import terminal ([Klaipėda LNG FSRU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da_LNG_FSRU)) in the Baltic region was built in the port of Klaipėda in 2014. The Klaipėda LNG terminal was called Independence, thus emphasising the aim to diversify the energy market of Lithuania. Norwegian company [Equinor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equinor) supplies 540 million cubic metres (19 billion cubic feet) of natural gas annually from 2015 until 2020.[^cite_note-302] The terminal is able to meet 100% of demand, and is projected to meet 90% of the demand in Latvia and Estonia.[^cite_note-303] [Gas Interconnection Poland–Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_Interconnection_Poland%E2%80%93Lithuania), also known as Lithuania–Poland pipeline, became operational in 2022.&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Lithuania)



Population density in Lithuania by elderships (administrative units)

Since the Neolithic period, the [demographics of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Lithuania) have stayed fairly homogeneous. There is a high probability that the inhabitants of present-day Lithuania have similar genetic compositions to their ancestors,[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] although without being actually isolated from them.[^cite_note-307] The population has little apparent genetic differences among ethnic subgroups.[^cite_note-308] A 2004 analysis of [MtDNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MtDNA) revealed that Lithuanians are genetically close to the [Slavic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_languages) and [Finno-Ugric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finno-Ugric_languages) speaking populations of northern and eastern Europe. [Y-chromosome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y_chromosome) SNP [haplogroup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haplogroup) analysis showed Lithuanians to be genetically closest to [Latvians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvians) and [Estonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonians).[^cite_note-309]&#x20;

The median age in 2022 was 44 years (male: 41, female: 47).[^cite_note-population2022-310] In 2021, the age structure of the population was as follows:&#x20;

- 0–14 years, 14.86% (male 214,113/female 203,117)

- 15–64 years: 65.19% (male 896,400/female 934,467)

- 65 years and over: 19.95% (male 195,269/female 365,014).

  [^cite_note-population2022-310]

Lithuania has a [sub-replacement fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility_rate): the [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) was 1.34 children born per woman in 2021, and the mean age of women at childbirth was 30.3 years. The average age of first childbirth for women was 28.2 years. The [human sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio) is male leaning for the age categories 15–44, with 1.0352 males for every female.[^cite_note-population2022-310] As of 2021, 25.6% of births were to unmarried women. The mean [age at first marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_at_first_marriage) in 2021 was 28.3 years for women and 30.5 years for men.[^cite_note-population2022-310]&#x20;

### Ethnic groups and languages

Main articles: [Lithuanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanians), [Ethnic minorities in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_minorities_in_Lithuania), and [Demographics of Lithuania § Languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Lithuania#Languages)

Ethnic Lithuanians make up about 80% of the country's population. In 2024, 82.6% of residents were ethnic Lithuanians. Several sizeable minorities exist, such as [Poles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_people) (6.3%), [Russians](59510.md) (5.0%), [Belarusians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusians) (2.1%) and [Ukrainians](145144.md) (1.7%).[^cite_note-TGKT21-312] [Poles in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poles_in_Lithuania) are the largest minority, concentrated in southeast Lithuania (the [Vilnius Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Region)), constituting majority in [Šalčininkai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0al%C4%8Dininkai_District_Municipality) (76.3%) and [Vilnius District Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_District_Municipality) (46.8%). [Russians in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians_in_Lithuania) are the second largest minority, concentrated in [Visaginas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visaginas_Municipality) (47.4%), [Zarasai District Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zarasai_District_Municipality) (17.2%) and [Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da) (16%).[^cite_note-TGKT21-312] About 2,250 [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people) live in Lithuania, mostly in Vilnius, [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas) and [Panevėžys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panev%C4%97%C5%BEys); their organisations are supported by the National Minority and Emigration Department.[^cite_note-313] For centuries, [Tatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipka_Tatars) and [Karaite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_Karaites) communities have lived in Lithuania. In 2021, there were around 2,150 registered Tatars and 196 Karaites in the country.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315]&#x20;

The official language is [Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_language), but in some areas there is a significant presence of minority languages such as [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language), [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language), [Belarusian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusian_language) and [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language). The greatest presence of minorities and the use of these languages are in Šalčininkai, Visaginas, and Vilnius District.[^cite_note-TGKT21-312] In 1941, the Jewish population reached its peak at approximately 250,000 people, making up about 10% of the population. Today, however, it has dwindled to a very small number. [Yiddish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yiddish) is spoken by members of the tiny remaining [Jewish community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Lithuania). The state laws guarantee education in minority languages, and there are numerous publicly funded schools in the areas populated by minorities, with Polish as the language of instruction being the most widely available.[^cite_note-316]&#x20;

According to the survey carried out within the framework of the [Lithuanian census of 2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_census_of_2021), 85.33% of the population speaks Lithuanian as their native language, 6.8% are native speakers of Russian and 5.1% of Polish. As of 2021, 60.6% of residents speak Russian as a foreign language, 31.1% English, 10.5% Lithuanian, 8% German, 7.9% Polish, 1.9% French, and 2.6% other languages.[^cite_note-317] Most schools teach English as the first foreign language, but students may also study German, French and Spanish; Russian is available as a second foreign language.[^cite_note-318] Around 85% of upper secondary school students were studying English in 2022,[^cite_note-319] and around 80% of people in the 15-19 age group reported knowing English in 2011.[^cite_note-320]&#x20;

### Urbanisation

See also: [List of cities in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Lithuania), [List of towns in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_towns_in_Lithuania), and [List of cities in the Baltic states by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_the_Baltic_states_by_population)

There has been a steady movement of population to the cities since the 1990s, encouraged by the planning of regional centres, such as [Alytus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alytus), [Marijampolė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marijampol%C4%97), [Utena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utena,_Lithuania), [Plungė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plung%C4%97), and [Mažeikiai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma%C5%BEeikiai). By the early 21st century, about two-thirds of the population lived in urban areas. As of 2021, 68.19% of the population lives in urban areas.[^cite_note-population2022-310] [Functional urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_urban_area) include [Vilnius](32597.md) (population 708,203), [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas) (population 391,153), and [Panevėžys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panev%C4%97%C5%BEys) (population 124,526).[^cite_note-FUA,Euro-311] The fDI of the Financial Times in their research *Cities and Regions of the Future* ranked Vilnius fourth in the mid-sized European cities category in the 2018–19 ranking, second in the 2022–23 ranking, second in 2023 ranking while the city claimed 24th spot in the worldwide overall ranking in 2021–22 and Vilnius county was ranked 10th in the small European regions category in 2018–19, fifth in 2022–23, fifth in 2023 rankings.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]&#x20;
