### Health

Main article: [Health in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Lithuania)



[Kaunas Clinics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_Clinics), a medical institution in Lithuania

Lithuania provides free state-funded healthcare to all citizens and registered long-term residents.[^cite_note-326] It co-exists with a significant private healthcare sector. In 2003–2012, the network of hospitals was restructured, as part of wider healthcare service reforms. It started in 2003–2005 with the expansion of ambulatory services and primary care.[^cite_note-327] In 2016, Lithuania ranked 27th in Europe in the [Euro health consumer index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro_health_consumer_index), a ranking of European healthcare systems based on waiting time, results and other indicators. Lithuania ranked 19th in the 2024 edition of the [World Happiness Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Happiness_Report).[^cite_note-\:1-328]&#x20;

As of 2023, [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) at birth was 76.0 (70.6 years for males and 81.6 for females)[^cite_note-329] and the [infant mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality) rate was 2.99 per 1,000 births.[^cite_note-330] The annual population growth rate increased by 0.3% in 2007. Lithuania has seen a dramatic rise in suicides in the 1990s.[^cite_note-who-suicide-data-331] The suicide rate has been constantly decreasing since, but it still remains the highest in the EU and one of the highest in the [OECD](33853117.md). The suicide rate as of 2019 is 20.2 per 100,000 people.[^cite_note-who-suicide-data-331] [Suicide in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_in_Lithuania) has been a subject of research, but the main reasons behind the high rate are psychological and economic, including social transformations and economic recessions, alcoholism, lack of tolerance in the society, and bullying.[^cite_note-lrt-suicide-reasons-332]&#x20;

By 2000, the vast majority of health care institutions were non-profit-making enterprises and a private sector developed, providing mostly outpatient services which are paid for out-of-pocket. The [Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Lithuania\)) also runs a few health care facilities and is involved in the running of the two major Lithuanian teaching hospitals. It is responsible for the State Public Health Centre which manages the public health network including ten county public health centres with their local branches. The ten [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Lithuania) run county hospitals and specialised health care facilities.[^cite_note-333]&#x20;

There is [Compulsory Health Insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Lithuania#Social_security_contributions) for the Lithuanian residents. There are 5 territorial health insurance funds, covering Vilnius, Kaunas, Klaipėda, Šiauliai and Panevėžys. Contributions for people who are economically active are 9% of income.[^cite_note-334] Emergency medical services are provided free of charge to all residents. Access to the secondary and tertiary care, such as hospital treatment, is normally via referral by a general practitioner.[^cite_note-335] Lithuania has one of the lowest health care prices in Europe.[^cite_note-336]&#x20;

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Lithuania)

See also: [Lithuanian mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_mythology)



[Hill of Crosses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_of_Crosses) near [Šiauliai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iauliai)



[Chapel of Saint Casimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapel_of_Saint_Casimir), the [patron saint](68055.md) of Lithuania and Lithuanian youth, with his [sarcophagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarcophagus) in the centre

According to the 2021 census, 74.2% of residents were Catholics.[^cite_note-religion-stats-3] Catholicism has been the main religion since the official [Christianisation of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianisation_of_Lithuania) in 1387. The Catholic Church was persecuted by the Russian Empire as part of the [Russification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russification) policies and by the Soviet Union as part of the overall [anti-religious campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_the_Soviet_Union). During the Soviet era, some priests actively led the resistance against the communist regime, as symbolised by the [Hill of Crosses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_of_Crosses) and exemplified by *[The Chronicle of the Catholic Church in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Chronicle_of_the_Catholic_Church_in_Lithuania)*.&#x20;

3.7% of the population are [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md), mainly among the Russian minority.[^cite_note-religion-stats-3] The community of [Old Believers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Believers) (0.6% of population) dates back to the 1660s. [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestants) are 0.8%, of which 0.6% are [Lutheran](23371382.md) and 0.2% are [Reformed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Evangelical_Reformed_Church). Before World War II, according to Losch (1932), the Lutherans were 3.3% of the total population.[^cite_note-lmaleidykla.lt-337] They were mainly Germans and [Prussian Lithuanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prussian_Lithuanians) in the [Klaipėda Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da_Region) (Memel territory). This population [fled or was expelled after the war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_and_expulsion_of_Germans_\(1944%E2%80%931950\)), and Protestantism is now mainly represented by ethnic Lithuanians throughout the northern and western parts of the country, as well as in large urban areas. Newly arriving [evangelical churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) have established missions since 1990.[^cite_note-338]&#x20;

Hinduism is a minority religion and a fairly recent development. Hinduism is spread by Hindu organisations: [ISKCON](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISKCON), [Sathya Sai Baba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sathya_Sai_Baba), [Brahma Kumaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brahma_Kumaris) and [Osho Rajneesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osho_Rajneesh). ISKCON (Lithuanian: *Krišnos sąmonės judėjimas*) is the largest and the oldest movement as the first Krishna followers date to 1979.[^cite_note-339] Brahma Kumaris maintains the Centre Brahma Kumaris in [Antakalnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antakalnis), Vilnius.&#x20;

The historical communities of [Lipka Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipka_Tatars) maintain [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Lithuania) as their religion. Lithuania was historically home to a significant [Jewish community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Lithuania) and was an important centre of Jewish scholarship and culture from the 18th century until the eve of World War II. Of the approximately 220,000 Jews who lived in Lithuania in June 1941, almost all were killed during [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust_in_Lithuania).[^cite_note-Bubnys_vanished219-340][^cite_note-341] The Lithuanian Jewish community (now with an umbrella body, the [Lithuanian Jewish Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Jewish_Community)), numbered about 4,000 at the end of 2009.[^cite_note-Stats-342]&#x20;

[Romuva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romuva_\(religion\)), the [neopagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neopagan) revival of the ancient religious practices, has gained popularity over the years. Romuva claims to continue living pagan traditions, which survived in folklore and customs.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] Romuva is a [polytheistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polytheism) pagan faith, which asserts the sanctity of nature and has elements of [ancestor worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestor_worship).[^cite_note-346] According to the [2001 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_census_of_2001), there were 1,270 people of Baltic faith in Lithuania.[^cite_note-347] That number jumped to 5,118 in the 2011 census and decreased to 3,917 in 2021 census.[^cite_note-osp.stat.gov.lt-348][^cite_note-349]&#x20;

### Education

Main article: [Education in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Lithuania)

See also: [List of universities and colleges in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Lithuania)



[Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University), one of the oldest universities in the region.

[^cite_note-350]

&#x20;It was established by [Stephen Báthory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_B%C3%A1thory), King of Poland and Grand Duke of Lithuania, in 1579.

The constitution mandates ten-year education ending at age 16 and guarantees a free public higher education for students deemed 'good'.[^cite_note-351] The [Ministry of Education and Science of the Republic of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Science_\(Lithuania\)) proposes national educational policies and goals that are then voted for in the Seimas. Laws govern long-term educational strategy along with general laws on standards for higher education, vocational training, law and science, adult education, and special education.[^cite_note-edu1-352] 5.4% of GDP or 15.4% of total public expenditure was spent for education in 2016.[^cite_note-edumonitor-353]&#x20;



[Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University) Life Sciences Center in the Sunrise Valley

According to the [World Bank](36752.md), the literacy rate among Lithuanians aged 15 years and older is 100%.[^cite_note-354] School attendance rates are above the EU average, and school leave is less common than in the EU. According to [Eurostat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurostat) Lithuania leads among other countries of the European Union in people with secondary education (93.3%).[^cite_note-355] Based on OECD data, Lithuania is among the top 5 countries in the world in postsecondary (tertiary) education attainment.[^cite_note-356] As of 2022, 58.15% of the population aged 25 to 34, and 33.28% of the population aged 55 to 64 had completed tertiary education.[^cite_note-357] The share of tertiary-educated 25–64-year-olds in STEM ([Science, technology, engineering, and mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science,_technology,_engineering,_and_mathematics)) fields in Lithuania were above the OECD average (29% and 26% respectively), similarly to business, administration and law (25% and 23% respectively).[^cite_note-358]&#x20;

The modern education system has multiple structural problems. Insufficient funding, quality issues, and decreasing student population are the most prevalent. Teacher salaries are below the EU average, despite significant increases since 2011.[^cite_note-359] Low teacher salaries was the primary reason behind national teacher strikes in 2014,[^cite_note-360] 2015,[^cite_note-361] and 2016.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] Salaries in the higher education sector are also low. Many professors have a second job to supplement their income.[^cite_note-364] PISA report from 2022 found that results in math, science and reading were around OECD average, after trailing the OECD average in earlier reports in 2010 and 2015, although the relative improvement was primarily driven by the decrease in performance in the other OECD countries as a result of COVID-19 pandemic.[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367] The population ages 6 to 19 has decreased by 36% between 2005 and 2015. As a result, the student-teacher ratio is decreasing and expenditure per student is increasing, but schools, particularly in rural areas, are forced into reorganisations and consolidations.[^cite_note-edumonitor-353] As with other Baltic nations, in particular Latvia, the large volume of higher education graduates within the country, coupled with the high rate of spoken second languages is contributing to an education [brain drain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brain_drain).&#x20;

As of 2008, there were 15 public and 6 private universities as well as 16 public and 11 private colleges.[^cite_note-368] [Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_University) is one of the [oldest universities in Northern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_universities_in_continuous_operation) and the largest university in Lithuania. [Kaunas University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_University_of_Technology) is the largest technical university in the Baltic States and the second largest university in Lithuania. In an attempt to reduce costs[^cite_note-369] and adapt to sharply decreasing number of high-school students,[^cite_note-370] parliament decided to reduce the number of universities.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] In early 2018, Lithuanian University of Educational Sciences and Aleksandras Stulginskis University were merged into [Vytautas Magnus University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Magnus_University).[^cite_note-373]&#x20;

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Lithuania)

See also: [List of Lithuanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Lithuanians)

### Lithuanian language



A priest, lexicographer [Konstantinas Sirvydas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantinas_Sirvydas) – cherisher of Lithuanian language in the 17th century



[Jonas Jablonskis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Jablonskis) is the father of standard Lithuanian language.

The Lithuanian language (*lietuvių kalba*) is recognised as one of the official languages of the European Union. There are about 2.96 million native Lithuanian speakers in Lithuania and about 200,000 abroad. Lithuanian is a [Baltic language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_languages), closely related to [Latvian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_language), although they are not [mutually intelligible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_intelligibility). It is written in an adapted version of the [Roman script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_alphabet). Lithuanian is believed to be the [linguistically most conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_\(language\)) living [Indo-European tongue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_languages), retaining many features of [Proto Indo-European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto_Indo-European_language).[^cite_note-zinkevicius-374] Lithuanian language studies are important for [comparative linguistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comparative_linguistics) and for reconstruction of [Proto-Indo-European language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Indo-European_language).[^cite_note-375]&#x20;



The earliest known Lithuanian glosses (between 1520 and 1530) written in the margins of [Johann Herolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Herolt) book *Liber Discipuli de eruditione Christifidelium*. Words: *teprÿdav\[ſ]ʒÿ* (let it strike), *vbagÿſte* (indigence).

There are two main dialects: [Aukštaitian dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auk%C5%A1taitian_dialect) and [Samogitian dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samogitian_dialect). Aukštaitian dialect is mainly used in the central, southern and eastern parts of Lithuania while Samogitian dialect is used in the western part of the country.[^cite_note-auto-376] The Samogitian dialect has many completely different words and is even considered a separate language by some linguists.[^cite_note-377] Nowadays, the distinguishing feature between the two dialects is the unequal pronunciation of accented and unaccented two-vowels uo and ie.[^cite_note-auto-376]&#x20;

The groundwork for written Lithuanian was laid in 16th and 17th centuries by Lithuanian noblemen and scholars, who promoted Lithuanian language, created dictionaries and published books – [Mikalojus Daukša](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikalojus_Dauk%C5%A1a), [Stanislovas Rapolionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanislovas_Rapolionis), [Abraomas Kulvietis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraomas_Kulvietis), [Jonas Bretkūnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Bretk%C5%ABnas), [Martynas Mažvydas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martynas_Ma%C5%BEvydas), [Konstantinas Sirvydas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantinas_Sirvydas), Simonas Vaišnoras-Varniškis.[^cite_note-378] The first grammar book of the Lithuanian language *[Grammatica Litvanica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammatica_Litvanica)* was published in Latin in 1653 by [Danielius Kleinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Klein_\(grammarian\)).&#x20;

[Jonas Jablonskis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Jablonskis)' works and activities are especially important for the Lithuanian literature moving from the use of dialects to a standard Lithuanian language. The linguistic material which he collected was published in the 20 volumes of [Academic Dictionary of Lithuanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_Dictionary_of_Lithuanian) and is still being used in research and in editing of texts and books. He also introduced the letter *ū* into Lithuanian writing.[^cite_note-379]&#x20;

### Literature



The first Lithuanian [printed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Printing) book, *[Catechism of Martynas Mažvydas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_Martynas_Ma%C5%BEvydas)* (1547, Königsberg)



Title page of *Radivilias* (1592, Vilnius). The poem celebrating commander [Mikołaj Radziwiłł the Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miko%C5%82aj_Radziwi%C5%82%C5%82_the_Red) (1512–84) and recounts the [famous victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ula) of [Lithuanian Armed Forces](17827.md) over Moscow troops (1564).

[^cite_note-380]

There is a great deal of [Lithuanian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_literature) written in [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin), the main scholarly language of the Middle Ages. The edicts of the King Mindaugas are the prime example of the literature of this kind. The Letters of Gediminas are another crucial heritage of the Lithuanian Latin writings.&#x20;

One of the first Lithuanian authors who wrote in Latin was early [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance) poet [Nicolaus Hussovianus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miko%C5%82aj_Hussowczyk). His poem *Carmen de statura, feritate ac venatione bisontis* (*A Song about the Appearance, Savagery and Hunting of the Bison*), published in 1523, describes the Lithuanian landscape, way of life and customs, touches on some actual political problems, and reflects the clash of paganism and Christianity. A person under the pseudonym [Michalo Lituanus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michalo_Lituanus) wrote a treatise *[De moribus tartarorum, lituanorum et moscorum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_moribus_tartarorum,_lituanorum_et_moscorum)* (*On the Customs of Tatars, Lithuanians and Muscovites*) in the middle of the 16th century. An extraordinary figure in the cultural life of Lithuania in the 16th century was the lawyer and poet of Spanish origin Petrus Roysius Maurus Alcagnicensis. The publicist, lawyer, and mayor of Vilnius, [Augustinus Rotundus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustinus_Rotundus) wrote a no longer existent history of Lithuania in Latin around 1560. loannes Radvanus, a humanist poet of the second half of the 16th century, wrote an epic poem imitating the [Aeneid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeneid) of [Vergil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgil). His *Radivilias*, intended to become the Lithuanian national epic, was published in Vilnius in 1588.[^cite_note-381]&#x20;

17th century Lithuanian scholars also wrote in Latin – [Kazimieras Kojelavičius-Vijūkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kazimieras_Kojelavi%C4%8Dius-Vij%C5%ABkas\&action=edit\&redlink=1), [Žygimantas Liauksminas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BDygimantas_Liauksminas) are known for their Latin writings in theology, rhetorics and music. [Albertas Kojalavičius-Vijūkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albertas_Kojalavi%C4%8Dius-Vij%C5%ABkas) wrote first printed Lithuanian history *Historia Lithuania*. Lithuanian literary works in the Lithuanian language started being published in the 16th century. In 1547 [Martynas Mažvydas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martynas_Ma%C5%BEvydas) compiled and published the first printed Lithuanian book *[Simple Words of Catechism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catechism_of_Martynas_Ma%C5%BEvydas)* (*Katekizmo paprasti žodžiai*). He was followed by [Mikalojus Daukša](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikalojus_Dauk%C5%A1a) with *Katekizmas*. In the 16th and 17th centuries, as in the whole Christian Europe, Lithuanian literature was primarily religious.&#x20;

The evolution of the old (14th–18th century) Lithuanian literature ends with [Kristijonas Donelaitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristijonas_Donelaitis), one of the most prominent authors of the [Age of Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment). Donelaitis' poem *Metai* (*[The Seasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Seasons_\(poem\))*) is a landmark of the Lithuanian fiction literature, written in [hexameter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hexameter).[^cite_note-INST-382] With a mix of [Classicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classicism), [Sentimentalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentimentalism_\(literature\)) and [Romanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism), the Lithuanian literature of the first half of the 19th century is represented by [Maironis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maironis), [Antanas Baranauskas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antanas_Baranauskas), [Simonas Daukantas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simonas_Daukantas), [Oscar Milosz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Milosz), and [Simonas Stanevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simonas_Stanevi%C4%8Dius).[^cite_note-INST-382] During the tsarist annexation of Lithuania in the 19th century, the [Lithuanian press ban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_press_ban) was implemented, which led to the formation of the *knygnešiai* ([Lithuanian book smugglers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_book_smugglers)) movement. 20th-century Lithuanian literature is represented by [Juozas Tumas-Vaižgantas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juozas_Tumas-Vai%C5%BEgantas), [Antanas Vienuolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antanas_Vienuolis), [Bernardas Brazdžionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardas_Brazd%C5%BEionis), [Antanas Škėma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antanas_%C5%A0k%C4%97ma), [Balys Sruoga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balys_Sruoga), [Vytautas Mačernis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Ma%C4%8Dernis) and [Justinas Marcinkevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justinas_Marcinkevi%C4%8Dius). In 21st century debuted [Kristina Sabaliauskaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristina_Sabaliauskait%C4%97), [Laura Sintija Černiauskaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Sintija_%C4%8Cerniauskait%C4%97), [Rūta Šepetys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruta_Sepetys).&#x20;

### Architecture

See also: [Lithuanian design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_design)

Several [famous Lithuania-related architects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Lithuanian_architects) are notable for their achievements in the field of architecture. [Johann Christoph Glaubitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johann_Christoph_Glaubitz), [Marcin Knackfus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcin_Knackfus), [Laurynas Gucevičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurynas_Gucevi%C4%8Dius) and [Karol Podczaszyński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol_Podczaszy%C5%84ski) were instrumental in introducing [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque_architecture) and [neoclassical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture) architectural movements to the Lithuanian architecture during the 17th to 19th centuries.[^cite_note-383] Vilnius is considered a capital of the Eastern Europe Baroque.[^cite_note-384] [Vilnius Old Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Old_Town) has many Baroque churches and other buildings and is a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md).[^cite_note-385] The [Vilnian Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnian_Baroque) style is named after Lithuania's capital city.[^cite_note-VB-386]&#x20;



[Church of St. Catherine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Catherine,_Vilnius) and [Church of St. Johns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_St._Johns,_Vilnius) (in the background, centre) are an examples of [Vilnian Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnian_Baroque)

Lithuania is known for [numerous castles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_castles_in_Lithuania). Some castles have been rebuilt or survive partially. Many Lithuanian nobles' historic [palaces and manor houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_palaces_and_manor_houses_in_Lithuania) have remained till the nowadays and were reconstructed.[^cite_note-387] Lithuanian village life has existed since the days of [Vytautas the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_the_Great). [Zervynos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zervynos) and [Kapiniškiai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapini%C5%A1kiai) are two of many [ethnographic villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnographic_village) in Lithuania.[^cite_note-388] [Rumšiškės](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rum%C5%A1i%C5%A1k%C4%97s) is an open space museum where old ethnographic architecture is preserved.&#x20;

During the [interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period), [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco), Lithuanian National Romanticism architectural style buildings were constructed in the temporary capital Kaunas. Its architecture is regarded as one of the finest examples of the European Art Deco and has received the [European Heritage Label](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Heritage_Label).[^cite_note-389]&#x20;

### Arts and museums

Main article: [List of museums in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Lithuania)



*[Fairy Tale of the Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairy_Tale_of_the_Kings)* (1908–1909) by [Mikalojus Konstantinas Čiurlionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikalojus_Konstantinas_%C4%8Ciurlionis)

The [Lithuanian Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Art_Museum) was founded in 1933 and is the largest museum of art conservation and display in Lithuania.[^cite_note-390] Among other important museums are the [Palanga Amber Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palanga_Amber_Museum), where amber pieces comprise a major part of the collection, National Gallery of Art, presenting collection of Lithuanian art of the 20th and 21st century, [National Museum of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Lithuania) presenting Lithuanian archaeology, history and ethnic culture. In 2018, two private museums were opened – [MO Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MO_Museum) devoted to modern and contemporary Lithuanian art and *Tartle*,[^cite_note-391] exhibiting a collection of Lithuanian art heritage and artefacts.&#x20;

Perhaps the most renowned figure in Lithuania's art community was 19th century composer [Mikalojus Konstantinas Čiurlionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikalojus_Konstantinas_%C4%8Ciurlionis). The [2420 Čiurlionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2420_%C4%8Ciurlionis) asteroid, identified in 1975, honors his achievements. The [M. K. Čiurlionis National Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M._K._%C4%8Ciurlionis_National_Art_Museum), as well as the only military museum in Lithuania, [Vytautas the Great War Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_the_Great_War_Museum), are located in Kaunas. [Franciszek Smuglewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciszek_Smuglewicz), [Jan Rustem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Rustem), [Józef Oleszkiewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%B3zef_Oleszkiewicz) and [Kanuty Rusiecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanuty_Rusiecki) are the most prominent Lithuanian painters of the 18th and 19th centuries.[^cite_note-392]&#x20;

### Theatre



[Lithuanian National Drama Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_National_Drama_Theatre)

Lithuania has theatres in Vilnius, Kaunas, Klaipėda and Panevėžys. These include [Lithuanian National Drama Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_National_Drama_Theatre), *[Keistuolių teatras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keistuoli%C5%B3_Teatras)* (Theatre of Freaks) in Vilnius, [Kaunas State Drama Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_State_Drama_Theatre), Theatre of Oskaras Koršunovas, Klaipėda Drama Theatre, Theatre of [Gytis Ivanauskas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gytis_Ivanauskas), Miltinis Drama Theatre in Panevėžys, The Doll's Theatre, [Old Theatre of Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Theatre_of_Vilnius).[^cite_note-393] Theatre festivals include *Sirenos* (Sirens), *TheATRIUM*, *Nerk į teatrą* (Dive into the Theatre).[^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396]&#x20;

Lithuanian theatre directors include [Eimuntas Nekrošius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eimuntas_Nekro%C5%A1ius), [Jonas Vaitkus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Vaitkus), Cezaris Graužinis, Gintaras Varnas, [Dalia Ibelhauptaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalia_Ibelhauptait%C4%97) and Artūras Areima. Actors include Dainius Gavenonis, Rolandas Kazlas, Saulius Balandis and Gabija Jaraminaitė.[^cite_note-397] Theatre director [Oskaras Koršunovas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oskaras_Kor%C5%A1unovas) was awarded the Swedish Commander Grand Cross – the [Order of the Polar Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_the_Polar_Star).[^cite_note-398]&#x20;

### Cinema

Main article: [Cinema of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Lithuania)



[Romuva Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romuva_Cinema), the oldest still operational [cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movie_theater) in Lithuania

On 28 July 1896, [Thomas Edison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Edison) performed a live photography session held in the Concerts Hall of the [Botanical Garden of Vilnius University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botanical_Garden_of_Vilnius_University). Similar American movies were available with the addition of special [phonograph records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phonograph_record) that also provided sound. In 1909, Lithuanian cinema pioneers [Antanas Račiūnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antanas_Ra%C4%8Di%C5%ABnas) and [Ladislas Starevich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ladislas_Starevich) released their first movies. Soon the Račiūnas' recordings of Lithuania's views became very popular among the [Lithuanian Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Americans) abroad. In 1925, Pranas Valuskis filmed movie *Naktis Lietuvoje* (Night in Lithuania) about [Lithuanian book smugglers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_book_smugglers) that left the first bright Lithuanian footprint in [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_\(film_industry\)). The most significant and mature Lithuanian American movie of the time *Aukso žąsis* (Golden goose) was created in 1965 by [Birutė Pūkelevičiūtė](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Birut%C4%97_P%C5%ABkelevi%C4%8Di%C5%ABt%C4%97\&action=edit\&redlink=1) that featured motifs from the [Brothers Grimm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothers_Grimm) fairy tales. In 1940, [Romuva Cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romuva_Cinema) was opened in [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas) and is the oldest operating cinema in Lithuania. After the occupation of the state, movies mostly were used for the [Soviet propaganda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_propaganda) purposes, nevertheless Almantas Grikevičius, Gytis Lukšas, Henrikas Šablevičius, Arūnas Žebriūnas, Raimondas Vabalas were able to overcome the obstacles and create valuable films. After the restoration of the independence, [Šarūnas Bartas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0ar%C5%ABnas_Bartas), [Audrius Stonys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audrius_Stonys), [Arūnas Matelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ar%C5%ABnas_Matelis), Audrius Juzėnas, [Algimantas Puipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algimantas_Puipa), [Janina Lapinskaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Janina_Lapinskait%C4%97\&action=edit\&redlink=1), Dijana and her husband Kornelijus Matuzevičius received success in international movie festivals.[^cite_note-399]&#x20;

### Music

Main article: [Music of Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Lithuania)





Lithuanians dancing at *Skamba skamba kankliai* festival and singing at [Lithuanian Song and Dance Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Song_Festival) in [Vingis Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vingis_Park)

Lithuanians are sometimes described as a "singing folk".[^cite_note-Opera-400] The [choral music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choral_music) traditions are prominent in the country. The long-standing *Dainų šventė* ([Lithuanian Song and Dance Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_Song_and_Dance_Festival)) is organized starting from 1924. Since 1990, the festival has been organised every four years and summons roughly 30,000 singers and folk dancers of various professional levels and age groups from across the country.[^cite_note-401] In 2008, Lithuanian Song and Dance Festival together with its [Latvian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_Song_and_Dance_Festival) and [Estonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonian_Song_Festival) versions was inscribed as UNESCO [Masterpiece of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity).[^cite_note-402]&#x20;

[Lithuanian folk music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_folk_music) belongs to Baltic music branch which is connected with Neolithic [corded ware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corded_ware) culture. Two instrument cultures meet in the areas inhabited by Lithuanians: stringed ([kanklių](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kankl%C4%97s)) and wind instrument cultures. Lithuanian folk music is archaic, mostly used for ritual purposes, containing elements of [paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paganism) faith. There are three ancient styles of singing in Lithuania connected with ethnographical regions: [monophony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monophony), [heterophony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heterophony) and [polyphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polyphony). Folk song genres: Sutartinės (Multipart Songs),[^cite_note-403] Wedding Songs, War-Historical Time Songs, Calendar Cycle and Ritual Songs and Work Songs.[^cite_note-404]&#x20;

The musical education and talent screening, especially for high-caliber individuals, is strong and holds an esteemed reputation.[^cite_note-Opera-400] Vilnius is the only city with three choirs laureates (Brevis, Jauna Muzika and Chamber Choir of the Conservatoire) at the [European Grand Prix for Choral Singing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Grand_Prix_for_Choral_Singing).[^cite_note-405] The biennial children's music festival [Dainų dainelė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dain%C5%B3_dainel%C4%97) has been organised since 1974, promoting singing traditions among the youth alongside other events such as [Laumės juosta](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Laum%C4%97s_juosta\&action=edit\&redlink=1). *[Gatvės muzikos diena](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gatv%C4%97s_muzikos_diena\&action=edit\&redlink=1)* (Street Music Day) gathers musicians of various genres annually.[^cite_note-406] [Music Information Centre Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_Information_Centre_Lithuania) collects, promotes and shares information on Lithuanian musical culture.&#x20;

Italian artists organised the first opera in Lithuania in 1636 at the [Palace of the Grand Dukes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Grand_Dukes_of_Lithuania) by the order of [Władysław IV Vasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_IV_Vasa).[^cite_note-407] Operas are staged at the [Lithuanian National Opera and Ballet Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_National_Opera_and_Ballet_Theatre) and also by independent troupe [Vilnius City Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_City_Opera). State also supports two full-time musical theatres of [Kaunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_State_Musical_Theatre) and [Klaipėda](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Klaip%C4%97da_State_Music_Theatre\&action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-408] After the restoration of independence, a lot of Lithuanian opera artists, such as [Virgilijus Noreika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgilijus_Noreika), [Violeta Urmana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violeta_Urmana), [Vytautas Juozapaitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Juozapaitis), [Asmik Grigorian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asmik_Grigorian), [Sigutė Stonytė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigut%C4%97_Stonyt%C4%97), etc. achieved international recognition and leading roles in the world's top theatres, while concurrently [Dalia Ibelhauptaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalia_Ibelhauptait%C4%97) earned acclaim as a director. According to *Financial Times*, roughly 20 Lithuanian top tier professionals are performing internationally per season.[^cite_note-Opera-400]&#x20;



Painter and composer [M.K. Čiurlionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikalojus_Konstantinas_%C4%8Ciurlionis)

[Mikalojus Konstantinas Čiurlionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikalojus_Konstantinas_%C4%8Ciurlionis) was a Lithuanian painter and composer. During his short life he created about 200 pieces of music. His works have influenced modern Lithuanian culture. His [symphonic poems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symphonic_poem) *In the Forest* (*Miške*) and *The Sea* (*Jūra*) were performed only posthumously. Čiurlionis contributed to symbolism and art nouveau and was representative of the fin de siècle epoch. He has been considered one of the pioneers of abstract art in Europe.[^cite_note-409] The [nations arts academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_M._K._%C4%8Ciurlionis_School_of_Art) is named after him.&#x20;

Modern classical composers emerged in 1970s – [Bronius Kutavičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronius_Kutavi%C4%8Dius), [Feliksas Bajoras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feliksas_Bajoras), [Osvaldas Balakauskas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osvaldas_Balakauskas), [Onutė Narbutaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onut%C4%97_Narbutait%C4%97), [Vidmantas Bartulis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidmantas_Bartulis) and others. Most of those composers explored archaic Lithuanian music and its harmonic combination with modern minimalism and neoromanticism.[^cite_note-410] Jazz scene was active even during the years of Soviet occupation. In 1970–71 the [Ganelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vyacheslav_Ganelin)/Tarasov/Chekasin trio established the Vilnius Jazz School.[^cite_note-411] Most known annual events are [Vilnius Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Jazz_Festival), [Kaunas Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaunas_Jazz), Birštonas Jazz.&#x20;

#### Rock and protest music

Main article: [Rock music in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_music_in_Lithuania)



Rock band [Antis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antis_\(band\)), which under firm [censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_in_the_Soviet_Union) actively mocked the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) regime by using [metaphors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metaphor) in their lyrics, during an [anti-Sovietism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Sovietism), [anti-communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communism) concert in 1987

After the Soviet reoccupation of Lithuania in 1944, the Soviet's censorship continued firmly controlling all artistic expressions in Lithuania, and any violations by criticizing the regime would immediately result in punishments.[^cite_note-412] The first local rock bands started to emerge around 1965 and included *Kertukai*, *Aitvarai* and *Nuogi ant slenksčio* in Kaunas, and [Kęstutis Antanėlis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C4%99stutis_Antan%C4%97lis), *Vienuoliai*, and *Gėlių Vaikai* in Vilnius, among others. Unable to express their opinions directly, the Lithuanian artists began organizing patriotic [Roko Maršai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roko_mar%C5%A1as) and were using [metaphors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metaphor) in their songs' lyrics, which were easily identified for their true meanings by the locals.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] [Postmodernist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Postmodernist) [rock](25423.md) band [Antis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antis_\(band\)) and its vocalist [Algirdas Kaušpėdas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algirdas_Kau%C5%A1p%C4%97das) were one of the most active performers who mocked the Soviet regime by using metaphors. For example, in the song *Zombiai* (Zombies), the band indirectly sang about the [Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Army) soldiers who occupied the state and its military base in [Ukmergė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukmerg%C4%97).[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] [Vytautas Kernagis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vytautas_Kernagis)' song *Kolorado vabalai* ([Colorado beetles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_potato_beetle)) was also a favourite due to its lyrics in which true meaning of the Colorado beetles was intended to be the Soviets decorated with the [Ribbons of Saint George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ribbon_of_Saint_George).[^cite_note-417]&#x20;

In the early independence years, rock band [Foje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foje) was particularly popular and gathered tens of thousands of spectators to the concerts.[^cite_note-418] After disbanding in 1997, Foje vocalist [Andrius Mamontovas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrius_Mamontovas) remained one of the most prominent Lithuanian performers and an active participant in various [charity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charity_\(practice\)) events.[^cite_note-419] [Marijonas Mikutavičius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marijonas_Mikutavi%C4%8Dius) is famous for creating unofficial Lithuania sport anthem *Trys milijonai* (Three millions) and official anthem of the [EuroBasket 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroBasket_2011) *Nebetyli sirgaliai* (English version was named *Celebrate Basketball*).[^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421]&#x20;

### Cuisine



Lithuanian dark [rye bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rye_bread)



*[Cepelinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cepelinai)*, a [potato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potato)-based dumpling dish characteristic of [Lithuanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_cuisine) with meat, [curd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curd) or mushrooms

[Lithuanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuanian_cuisine) features the products suited to the cool and moist northern climate of Lithuania: barley, potatoes, rye, beets, greens, berries, and mushrooms are locally grown, and dairy products are one of its specialties. Fish dishes are very popular in the coastal region.[^cite_note-422] Since it shares its climate and agricultural practices with Northern Europe, Lithuanian cuisine has some similarities to Scandinavian cuisine. Nevertheless, it has its own distinguishing features, which were formed by a variety of influences during the country's long and difficult history.&#x20;

Dairy products include white cottage cheese (*varškės sūris*), curd (*varškė*), soured milk (*rūgpienis*), sour cream (*grietinė*), butter (*sviestas*), and sour cream butter *kastinis*. Traditional meat products are usually seasoned, matured and smoked – smoked sausages (*dešros*), lard (*lašiniai*), *[skilandis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skilandis)*, smoked ham (*kumpis*). Soups (*sriubos*)—boletus soup (*baravykų sriuba*), cabbage soup (*kopūstų sriuba*), beer soup (*alaus sriuba*), milk soup (*pieniška sriuba*), cold-beet soup (*šaltibarščiai*)—and various kinds of porridges (*košės*) are part of tradition and daily diet. Freshwater fish, herring, wild berries and mushrooms, honey are highly popular diet to this day.[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424]&#x20;



Lithuania has longlasting [beer brewing traditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_in_Lithuania).

One of the oldest and most fundamental food products is rye bread. Rye bread is eaten every day for breakfast, lunch and dinner. Bread played an important role in family rituals and agrarian ceremonies.[^cite_note-425]&#x20;

Lithuanians and other nations that once formed part of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania share many dishes and beverages. [German traditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_cuisine) also influenced Lithuanian cuisine, introducing pork and potato dishes, such as potato pudding (*kugelis* or kugel) and [potato sausages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kishka_\(food\)) (*vėdarai*), as well as the baroque tree cake known as *[Šakotis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0akotis)*. The most exotic of all the influences is Eastern (Karaite) cuisine – the *[kibinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kibinai)* are popular in Lithuania. Noblemen usually hired French chefs, so [French cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_cuisine) influence came to Lithuania in this way.[^cite_note-426]&#x20;

Balts were using [mead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mead) (*[midus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midus)*) for thousands of years.[^cite_note-427] Beer (*alus*) is the most common alcoholic beverage. Lithuania has a long farmhouse [beer tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_in_Lithuania), first mentioned in 11th century chronicles. Beer was brewed for ancient Baltic festivities and rituals.[^cite_note-428] Farmhouse brewing survived to a greater extent in Lithuania than anywhere else, and through accidents of history the Lithuanians then developed a commercial brewing culture from their unique farmhouse traditions.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] Lithuania is top 5 by consumption of beer per capita in Europe in 2015, counting 75 active breweries, 32 of them are microbreweries.[^cite_note-431]&#x20;

Eight Lithuanian restaurants are listed in the White Guide Baltic Top 30.[^cite_note-432] The local "*30 geriausių restoranų*" guide lists top domestic places,[^cite_note-433] and Lithuanian restaurants appear in the 2024 [Michelin Guide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelin_Guide).[^cite_note-434]&#x20;

### Media

Main article: [Mass media in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Lithuania)

The constitution provides for [freedom of speech](21401843.md) and [press](105323.md), and the government generally respects these rights in practice. An independent press, an effective judiciary, and a functioning democratic political system combine to promote these freedoms. However, the constitutional definition of freedom of expression does not protect certain acts, such as incitement to national, racial, religious, or social hatred, violence and discrimination, or slander, and disinformation. It is a crime to deny or "grossly trivialise" Soviet or Nazi German crimes against Lithuania or its citizens, or to deny genocide, crimes against humanity, or war crimes.[^cite_note-435]&#x20;

In 2021, the best-selling daily national newspapers were *[Lietuvos rytas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_rytas)* (5.4% of all weekly readers), *[Vakaro žinios](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Vakaro_%C5%BEinios\&action=edit\&redlink=1)* (3.2%), *[Kauno diena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kauno_diena)* (2.9%). Best-selling weekly newspapers were *[Savaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Savait%C4%97_\(1999\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1)* (16.5%), *[Žmonės](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=%C5%BDmon%C4%97s_\(%C5%BEurnalas\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1)* (8.4%), *Prie kavos* (4.1%), *[Savaitgalis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_rytas)* (3.9%) and *[Verslo žinios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verslo_%C5%BEinios)* (3.2%).[^cite_note-Kantar21-436] In 2021, the most popular national television channels were [TV3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV3_\(Lithuanian_TV_channel\)) (34.6% of the daily audience), [LNK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LNK_\(Lithuanian_TV_channel\)) (32.3%), [Lithuanian National Radio and Television](2672756.md) (31.6%), [BTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BTV_\(Lithuanian_TV_channel\)) (17.3%), [Lietuvos rytas TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lietuvos_rytas_TV) (16.2%), [TV6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV6_\(Lithuanian_TV_channel\)) (15.3%).[^cite_note-Kantar21-436] The most popular radio stations were [M-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M-1_\(Lithuanian_radio_station\)) (14.5% of daily listeners), *Lietus* (12.7%), *[Radiocentras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiocentras_\(Lithuanian_radio_station\))* (9.1%) and *[LRT Radijas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LRT_Radijas)* (8.5%).[^cite_note-Kantar21-436]&#x20;

### Public holidays and festivals

Main article: [Public holidays in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Lithuania)

As a result of a thousand-years history, Lithuania has two [national days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_day). The first one is the [Statehood Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statehood_Day_\(Lithuania\)) on 6 July, marking the establishment of the medieval Kingdom of Lithuania in 1253. The creation of modern Lithuanian state is commemorated on 16 February as a [Lithuanian State Reestablishment Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_of_Independence_of_Lithuania) on which independence from Russia and Germany was declared in 1918. [Joninės](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Jonas%27s_Festival) (previously known as *Rasos*) is a public holiday with pagan roots that celebrates a solstice. There are 13 public holidays (which come with a day off).[^cite_note-437]&#x20;

[Kaziuko mugė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaziuko_mug%C4%97) is an annual fair held since the beginning of the 17th century that commemorates the anniversary of [Saint Casimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Casimir)'s death and gathers thousands of visitors and many craftsmen. Other notable festivals are [Vilnius International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_International_Film_Festival), [Kauno Miesto Diena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kauno_Miesto_Diena), [Klaipėda Sea Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klaip%C4%97da_Sea_Festival), [Mados infekcija](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Mados_infekcija\&action=edit\&redlink=1), [Vilnius Book Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Book_Fair), [Vilnius Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilnius_Marathon), [Devilstone Open Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devilstone_Open_Air), [Apuolė 854](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apuol%C4%97_854\&action=edit\&redlink=1), [Great Žemaičių Kalvarija Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_%C5%BDemai%C4%8Di%C5%B3_Kalvarija_Festival).&#x20;

### Sports

Main articles: [Sport in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Lithuania) and [Basketball in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_in_Lithuania)

Basketball is the most popular and national sport of Lithuania. The [national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_men%27s_national_basketball_team) has won the [EuroBasket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroBasket) on three occasions ([1937](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobasket_1937), [1939](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobasket_1939) and [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobasket_2003)), as well as 8 other medals in the Eurobasket, the [World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_World_Championship) and the [Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_Summer_Olympics). 76% of the population watched the men's national team games live in 2014.[^cite_note-438] Lithuania hosted the Eurobasket in 1939 and [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobasket_2011). The historic basketball team [BC Žalgiris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BC_%C5%BDalgiris), from Kaunas, won the European basketball league [Euroleague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euroleague) in 1999. Lithuania has [produced a number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Lithuanian_NBA_players) of [NBA](22093.md) players, including [Naismith Memorial Basketball Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naismith_Memorial_Basketball_Hall_of_Fame) inductees [Arvydas Sabonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arvydas_Sabonis) and [Šarūnas Marčiulionis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0ar%C5%ABnas_Mar%C4%8Diulionis),[^cite_note-439] and current NBA players [Jonas Valančiūnas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonas_Valan%C4%8Di%C5%ABnas), [Domantas Sabonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domantas_Sabonis).[^cite_note-440]&#x20;



[Lithuania men's national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_men%27s_national_basketball_team) is ranked 10th worldwide in [FIBA Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Rankings).

Lithuania has won a total of [26 medals at the Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_at_the_Olympics), including 6 gold medals in athletics, [modern pentathlon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_pentathlon), shooting, and swimming. Other Lithuanians won Olympic medals representing Soviet Union. Discus thrower [Virgilijus Alekna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virgilijus_Alekna) is the most successful Olympic athlete of independent Lithuania, having won gold medals in the [2000 Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympics) and [2004 Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Summer_Olympics) games, as well as a bronze in [2008 in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Summer_Olympics) and numerous [World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAAF_World_Championships_in_Athletics) medals. A gold medal was won by 15-year-old swimmer [Rūta Meilutytė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C5%ABta_Meilutyt%C4%97) at the [2012 London Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics).&#x20;

Lithuania hosted the [2021 FIFA Futsal World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_FIFA_Futsal_World_Cup), the first time Lithuania had hosted a FIFA tournament.[^cite_note-441]&#x20;

Few Lithuanian athletes have found success in winter sports, although facilities are provided by several ice rinks and skiing slopes, including [Snow Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snow_Arena), the first indoor ski slope in the Baltics.[^cite_note-442] In 2018 [Lithuania men's national ice hockey team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania_men%27s_national_ice_hockey_team) won gold medals at the [2018 IIHF World Championship Division I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_IIHF_World_Championship_Division_I).[^cite_note-443]&#x20;

[^cite_note-location-28]: &#x20;Various sources classify Lithuania differently for statistical and other purposes. For example, United Nations,<sup>[\[17\]](#cite_note-UN-20)</sup> and [Eurovoc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovoc) (which additionally classifies Lithuania as central and eastern European country),<sup>[\[18\]](#cite_note-21)</sup> among others, classify it as northern Europe. The [European Commission](9974.md), [European Bank for Reconstruction and Development](37963.md) and the [Columbia Encyclopedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_Encyclopedia) place Lithuania in central Europe.<sup>[\[19\]](#cite_note-22)</sup><sup>[\[20\]](#cite_note-23)</sup><sup>[\[21\]](#cite_note-24)</sup> The [CIA World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook)<sup>[\[22\]](#cite_note-CIA-25)</sup> classifies it as eastern Europe, and *[Encyclopædia Britannica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica)* locates it in northeastern Europe.<sup>[\[23\]](#cite_note-26)</sup> Usage varies greatly, and controversially,<sup>[\[24\]](#cite_note-27)</sup> in press sources.

[^cite_note-5]: &#x20;Veser, Ernst (23 September 1997). ["Semi-Presidentialism-Duverger's Concept – A New Political System Model"](https://web.archive.org/web/20210424202059/https://www.rchss.sinica.edu.tw/publication/ebook/journal/11-01-1999/11_1_2.pdf) (PDF) (in English and Chinese). Department of Education, School of Education, [University of Cologne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cologne). pp. 39–60. Archived from [the original](http://www.rchss.sinica.edu.tw/publication/ebook/journal/11-01-1999/11_1_2.pdf) (PDF) on 24 April 2021. Retrieved 23 August 2017. Duhamel has developed the approach further: He stresses that the French construction does not correspond to either parliamentary or the presidential form of government, and then develops the distinction of 'système politique' and 'régime constitutionnel'. While the former comprises the exercise of power that results from the dominant institutional practice, the latter is the totality of the rules for the dominant institutional practice of the power. In this way, France appears as 'presidentialist system' endowed with a 'semi-presidential regime' (1983: 587). By this standard he recognizes Duverger's *pléiade* as semi-presidential regimes, as well as Poland, Romania, Bulgaria and Lithuania (1993: 87).

[^cite_note-Dual-7]: &#x20;[Shugart, Matthew Søberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_S%C3%B8berg_Shugart) (December 2005). ["Semi-Presidential Systems: Dual Executive And Mixed Authority Patterns"](https://doi.org/10.1057%2Fpalgrave.fp.8200087). *French Politics*. **3** (3). [Palgrave Macmillan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palgrave_Macmillan) Journals: 323–351. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\)):[10.1057/palgrave.fp.8200087](https://doi.org/10.1057%2Fpalgrave.fp.8200087). [ISSN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISSN_\(identifier\)) [1476-3419](https://search.worldcat.org/issn/1476-3419). A pattern similar to the French case of compatible majorities alternating with periods of cohabitation emerged in Lithuania, where Talat-Kelpsa (2001) notes that the ability of the Lithuanian president to influence government formation and policy declined abruptly when he lost the sympathetic majority in parliament.

Lithuania last night became the first republic to break away from the Soviet Union, by proclaiming the restoration of its pre-war independence. The newly-elected parliament, 'reflecting the people's will,' decreed the restoration of 'the sovereign rights of the Lithuanian state, infringed by alien forces in 1940,' and declared that from that moment Lithuania was again an independent state

[^cite_note-85]: &#x20;Martha Brill Olcott (1990). Retrieved 18 November 2018. For over two years Lithuania has been moving toward reclaiming its independence. This drive reached a crescendo on 11 March 1990, when the Supreme Soviet of Lithuania declared the republic no longer bound by Soviet law. The act reasserted the independence Lithuania had declared more than seventy years before, a declaration unilaterally annulled by the U.S.S.R. in 1940 when it annexed Lithuania as the result of a pact between Stalin and Hitler.
